<!-- thm-1: body_md -->
\label{thm1.1}
Suppose that $(\Omega_{in},\Theta_{in})$ is the initial data for \eqref{eq:1.4} with $\mathbb{D}=\mathbb{T}$. There exists a small constant $\varepsilon_0>0$ independent of $\nu$ such that if
\begin{equation}\label{1.4}
    \left\|\Omega_{in}\right\|_{H^3_{x,y}}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\Theta_{in}\right\|_{H^4_{x,y}}\leq \varepsilon_0\nu^{\frac13},
\end{equation}
then for $0<\delta\ll 1$ (depending only on universal constants), $t\geq 0$ and $0<\nu\leq 1$,  the corresponding solution $(\Omega,\Theta)$ to \eqref{eq:1.4} satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{1.6}
\left\|\Omega_{\neq}(t)\right\|_{L^2_{x,y}}+\left\|\Theta_{\neq}(t)\right\|_{L^2_{x,y}}\lesssim \varepsilon_0{\rm e}^{-\delta\nu^{\frac13} t},   
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}\label{1.7}
    \left\|U_{\neq}(t)\right\|_{L^2_{x,y}}\lesssim \varepsilon_0(1+t)^{-1}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\delta}{2}\nu^{\frac13} t}.
\end{equation}
<!-- end thm-1 -->
<!-- rem-1: body_md -->
Compared with the results in Zhang and Zi \cite{ZhangZi}, we successfully extend the whole-space case ($y\in \mathbb{R}$) to the case with boundaries, and consider two different types of domains
separately.
 According to \cite{wei-zhang2023,wei-zhang2026}, the stability threshold for $\Omega_{in}$ should be optimal. 
Due to the presence of the buoyancy term $ -\partial_x \Theta$ in the vorticity equation, and since the multiplier method cannot be applied as in the whole-space case, the stability threshold for the temperature is larger.
<!-- end rem-1 -->
<!-- thm-2: body_md -->
\label{thm1.2}
Suppose that $(\Omega_{in},\Theta_{in})$ is the initial data for \eqref{eq:1.4} with $\mathbb{D}=\mathbb{R}$. There exists a small constant $\varepsilon_0>0$ independent of $\nu$ such that if
\begin{equation}\label{1.8}
    \|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3_{x,y}\cap L^1_xH^3_{y}}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4_{x,y}\cap L^1_xH^4_{y}}\leq \varepsilon_0\nu^{\frac13},
\end{equation}
then for $0<\delta\ll 1$, $t\geq 0$ and $0<\nu\leq 1$,  the solution $(\Omega,\Theta)$ to \eqref{eq:1.4} satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{1.9}
\left\|(1+\nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^\delta\Omega(t)\right\|_{L^2_{x,y}}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left\|(1+\nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^\delta\Theta(t)\right\|_{L^2_{x,y}}\lesssim \varepsilon_0,   
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}\label{1.10}
    \left\|(1+\nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^\delta \mathcal{M}_2 U(t)\right\|_{L^2_{t}L^2_{x,y}}\lesssim \varepsilon_0.
\end{equation}
Here $\mathcal{M}_1=\mathcal{M}_1(\partial_x)$ and $\mathcal{M}_2(\partial_x)$ are the Fourier multipliers, which are defined as (see \cite{miao2025})
\begin{align}\label{M}
\mathcal{M}_1(k)=|k|^{\frac23}\chi+1-\chi, \quad \mathcal{M}_2 (k)=|k|^{\frac12}+|k|,
\end{align}
where $\chi$ is a cut-off function satisfying $\chi(k)=\begin{cases}
1 & |k|\leq \frac12, \\
-|k|^2-\frac{|k|}{2}+\frac{3}{2} & \frac12<|k|< 1, \\
0 & |k|\geq 1.
\end{cases}$
<!-- end thm-2 -->
<!-- prop-1: body_md -->
\label{p2.1}
Under the conditions of Theorem \ref{thm1.1}, the solution $(\omega_{a},\theta_{a})$ of \eqref{2.7}  satisfies that for $t \geq 0$,
\begin{align}
& \|\partial_x \theta_{a}(t)\|_{L^\infty} + \|(\partial_y + t\partial_x)\theta_{a}(t)\|_{L^\infty} \lesssim {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}, \label{2.10}\\
& \|\partial_x \omega_{a}(t)\|_{L^\infty} + \|(\partial_y + t\partial_x)\omega_{a}(t)\|_{L^\infty} \lesssim {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}), \label{2.11}\\
& \|\partial_x^j u_{a}^{(2)}(t)\| + \|\partial_x^j(u_{a}^{(1)} - t u_{a}^{(2)})(t)\| \notag\\ &\ \ \lesssim (1+t)^{-2} {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}),\ j=0,1,2,3, 4,\label{2.12}\\
& (1+t) \|\partial_x^ju_{a}^{(2)}(t)\|_{L^\infty} + \|\partial_x^ju_{a}^{(1)}(t)\|_{L^\infty} \notag\\ 
&\ \ \lesssim (1+t)^{-1} {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t}(\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}), \ j=0,1,2,\label{2.13}\\
& \|Er^1(t)\| \lesssim \nu (1+t){\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}) \notag\\
&\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +  (1+t)^{-2} {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4})^2,\label{2.14}\\
& \|Er^2(t)\|\lesssim  \nu (1+t) {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} \notag\\
&\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \  +  (1+t)^{-2} {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}(\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}).\label{2.15}
\end{align}
<!-- end prop-1 -->
<!-- prop-2: body_md -->
\label{p2.2}
Under the conditions of Theorem \ref{thm1.1}, the solution $(\omega,\theta)$ to \eqref{2.9} satisfies
that for $ t \geq 0 $,
\begin{equation}\label{222}
\|\omega_{\neq}(t)\|+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\theta_{\neq}(t)\| \lesssim {\rm e}^{-\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} (\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} \|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^3}).
\end{equation}
<!-- end prop-2 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
By using \eqref{2.12}, one has
\begin{align*}
\|u_{a}(t)\| &\leq \|u_{a}^{(1)}(t)\| + \|u_{a}^{(2)}(t)\| \\
&\leq (1 + t) \|u_{a}^{(2)}(t)\| + \|(u_{a}^{(1)} - t u_{a}^{(2)})(t)\| \\
&\lesssim(1 + t)^{-1} {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}).
\end{align*}
By applying \eqref{2.10}--\eqref{2.11} and $P_0 \theta_{a}=P_0 \omega_{a} = 0$, we get
\begin{equation}\notag
\|\theta_{a}(t)\| \leq \|\partial_x \theta_{a}(t)\| \lesssim \|\partial_x \theta_{a}(t)\|_{L^\infty} \lesssim {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4},
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}\notag
\|\omega_{a}(t)\| \leq \|\partial_x \omega_{a}(t)\| \lesssim \|\partial_x \omega_{a}(t)\|_{L^\infty} \lesssim {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}).
\end{equation}
Notice that $u = (\partial_y, -\partial_x) \Delta^{-1} \omega$. Then we use Proposition~\ref{p2.2} to get
\begin{equation}\notag
\|\theta_{\neq}(t)\| \lesssim {\rm e}^{-\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} (\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} \|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^3}),
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}\notag
\|u_{\neq}(t)\| \lesssim \|\omega_{\neq}(t)\| \lesssim {\rm e}^{-\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} (\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} \|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^3}).
\end{equation}
Thanks to \eqref{2.5}, we have  
\begin{equation}\label{2.16}
 U_{\neq} = P_{\neq} u_{ob},\   \Omega_{\neq} = P_{\neq} \omega_{ob}, \ \Theta_{\neq} = P_{\neq} \theta_{ob}. 
\end{equation}
Thus, it holds that
\begin{align}\label{2.17}
\|\Theta_{\neq}(t)\| &= \|P_{\neq} \theta_{ob}(t)\| \leq \|\theta_{a}(t)\| + \|P_{\neq} \theta(t)\| \notag\\
&\lesssim {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}+{\rm e}^{-\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} (\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} \|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^3})\notag\\
&\lesssim {\rm e}^{-\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t}(\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}),
\end{align}
\begin{align}\label{2.18}
\|\Omega_{\neq}(t)\| &= \|P_{\neq} \omega_{ob}(t)\| \leq \|\omega_{a}(t)\| + \|P_{\neq} \omega(t)\| \notag\\
&\lesssim {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4})+{\rm e}^{-\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} (\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} \|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^3})\notag\\
&\lesssim {\rm e}^{-\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t}(\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}),
\end{align}
and
\begin{align}\label{2.19}
\|U_{\neq}(t)\| &= \|P_{\neq} u_{ob}(t)\| \leq \|u_{a}(t)\| + \|P_{\neq} u(t)\| \notag\\
&\lesssim(1 + t)^{-1} {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3} +  {\rm e}^{-\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}) \notag\\
&\lesssim(1 + t)^{-1} {\rm e}^{-\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t / 2} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}).
\end{align}
By combining \eqref{2.17}--\eqref{2.19}, we get \eqref{1.6}--\eqref{1.7}, and thus complete the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm1.1}.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- prop-3: body_md -->
\label{linear}
Under the conditions of Theorem \ref{thm1.2}, the solution $(\omega_{a},\theta_{a})$ to \eqref{a3.3}  satisfies for $m\geq 0$ that
\begin{align}
&\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}[\theta_{a},(\partial_y+t\partial_x)\theta_{a}](t)\|\lesssim_m (1+\nu t^3)^{-\frac14}{\rm e}^{-\nu t}\mathcal{E}_{2,in}, \label{a3.6}\\
&\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}\partial_x\theta_{a}(t)\|\lesssim_m (1+\nu t^3)^{-\frac34}{\rm e}^{-\nu t}\mathcal{E}_{2,in},\label{a3.7}\\
&\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}\partial_x\theta_{a}(t)\|_{L^\infty}\lesssim_m (1+\nu t^3)^{-1}{\rm e}^{-\nu t}\mathcal{E}_{2,in}, \label{a3.8}\\
&\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}(\partial_y+t\partial_x)\theta_{a}(t)\|_{L^\infty}\lesssim_m (1+\nu t^3)^{-\frac12}{\rm e}^{-\nu t}\mathcal{E}_{2,in}, \label{a3.9}\\
&\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}\partial^2_x\theta_{a}(t)\|_{L^\infty}\lesssim_m (\nu t^3)^{-\frac13}(1+\nu t^3)^{-\frac76}{\rm e}^{-\nu t}\mathcal{E}_{2,in}, \label{a3.10}
\end{align}
\begin{align}
&\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}[\omega_{a},(\partial_y+t\partial_x)\omega_{a}](t)\|\lesssim_m (1+\nu t^3)^{-\frac14}{\rm e}^{-\nu t}\mathcal{E}_{in}, \label{paxpayomL L2}\\
&\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}\partial_x\omega_{a}(t)\|\lesssim_m (1+\nu t^3)^{-\frac34}{\rm e}^{-\nu t}\mathcal{E}_{in},\label{paxomL2}\\
&\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}\partial_x\omega_{a}(t)\|_{L^\infty}\lesssim_m (1+\nu t^3)^{-1}{\rm e}^{-\nu t}\mathcal{E}_{in}, \label{omL Linfty}\\
&\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}(\partial_y+t\partial_x)\omega_{a}(t)\|_{L^\infty}\lesssim_m (1+\nu t^3)^{-\frac12}{\rm e}^{-\nu t}\mathcal{E}_{in}, \label{paxomL Linfty}\\
&\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}\partial^2_x\omega_{a}(t)\|_{L^\infty}\lesssim_m (\nu t^3)^{-\frac13}(1+\nu t^3)^{-\frac76}{\rm e}^{-\nu t}\mathcal{E}_{in}, \label{paxxxomL Linfty}
\end{align}
and 
\begin{align}
&\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}[u_a^{(1)},(u_a^{(1)}-tu_a^{(2)})](t)\|_{L^\infty}\lesssim_m (1+t)^{-1}{\rm e}^{-\nu t}\mathcal{E}_{in},\label{u1LLinfty}\\
&\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}u_a^{(2)}(t)\|_{L^\infty}\lesssim_m(1+t)^{-2}\ln(1+t){\rm e}^{-\nu t}\mathcal{E}_{in},\label{u12L22}
\end{align}
\begin{align} \label{a3.18}
&\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}E_r^2(t)\|\notag\\
\lesssim_m& \Big((1+t)^{-1}(1+\nu t^3)^{-\frac14} \mathcal{E}_{in} +\nu(1+\nu t^3)^{-\frac14}+\nu^{\frac23}(1+\nu t^3)^{-\frac{5}{12}}\Big){\rm e}^{-\nu t}\mathcal{E}_{2,in},
\end{align}
\begin{align} \label{ErL2}
&\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}E_r^1(t)\|\notag\\
\lesssim_m& \Big((1+t)^{-1}(1+\nu t^3)^{-\frac14} \mathcal{E}_{in} +\nu(1+\nu t^3)^{-\frac14}+\nu^{\frac23}(1+\nu t^3)^{-\frac{5}{12}}\Big){\rm e}^{-\nu t}\mathcal{E}_{in}.
\end{align}
<!-- end prop-3 -->
<!-- prop-4: body_md -->
\label{ap3.2}
Under the conditions of Theorem \ref{thm1.2}, the solution $(\omega,\theta)$ to \eqref{a3.4}--\eqref{a3.5}  satisfies for $0\leq t\leq \nu^{-\frac16}$,
\begin{align}\label{a3.20}
\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\omega(t)\|+\nu^{-\frac12}\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\theta(t)\|\lesssim \nu^{\frac13}\ln(1+\nu^{-1})\mathcal{E}_{in}.
\end{align}
For $t\geq \nu^{-\frac16}$,
\begin{equation}\label{a3.20a}
    \|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\omega(t)\|+\nu^{-\frac12}\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\theta(t)\|+\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\mathcal{M}_2u(t)\|_{L^2L^2}\lesssim \mathcal{E}_{in}.
\end{equation}
<!-- end prop-4 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
Due to the fact that $\Omega=\omega_a+\omega$ and $\Theta=\theta_{a}+\theta$, we can conclude
\begin{align*}
\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\Omega\|&\leq \|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\omega_a\|+\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\omega\|,\\
\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\Theta\|&\leq \|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\theta_{a}\|+\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\theta\|.
\end{align*}
By taking $m=\delta$ in \eqref{a3.6} and \eqref{paxpayomL L2} respectively, we obtain
\begin{align*}
\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\omega_a\|\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{in},\ \ \|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\theta_{a}\|\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{2,in}.
\end{align*}
By Proposition \ref{ap3.2}, we obtain
\begin{align*}
\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\omega\|+\nu^{-\frac12}\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\theta\|\lesssim \mathcal{E}_{in}.
\end{align*}
Notice that $\mathcal{E}_{in}=\mathcal{E}_{1,in}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,in}$, we can deduce \eqref{1.9}.

For the inviscid damping estimate \eqref{1.10}, we first use $U=u_{a}+u$ to obtain
\begin{align}\label{esti u}
&\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\mathcal{M}_2U\|_{L^2L^2}\notag\\
\leq &\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}   t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\mathcal{M}_2u_{a}\|_{L^2L^2}+\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13} t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\mathcal{M}_2u\|_{L^2L^2}.
\end{align}
Thanks to Plancherel's formula, $\mathcal{M}_1(k)\leq 3|k|^{\frac23}$, \eqref{k>1, psiL2}--\eqref{xi>1 xi4pay+xitpsiL2}, \eqref{esti4 psiL2}--\eqref{xi<1 pay+xitpsiL2}, \eqref{a3.26}, we get
\begin{align}\label{uLneq}
&\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\mathcal{M}_2u_{a}\|^2_{L^2L^2}\notag\\
 \lesssim &\int^{\infty}_0\int_{|k|\geq 1}(1+ \nu^{\frac13}|k|^{\frac23} t)^{2\delta}|k|^2\big((|k|+|k|t)\|\phi_{a}\|_{L^2_y}+\|(\partial_y+ikt)\phi_{a}\|_{L^2_y}\big)^2 {\rm d} k{\rm d} t\notag\\
 &+\int^{\infty}_0\int_{|k|\leq 1}(1+ \nu^{\frac13} |k|^{\frac23} t)^{2\delta}|k|((|k|+|k|t)\|\phi_{a}\|_{L^2_y}+\|(\partial_y+ikt)\phi_{a}\|_{L^2_y})^2 {\rm d} k{\rm d} t\notag\\
\lesssim &\mathcal{E}_{in}^2\int^{\infty}_0 (1+t)^{-\frac32} {\rm d} t\lesssim \mathcal{E}_{in}^2,
\end{align}
where we used
\begin{align*}
\int_{|k|\leq 1}\frac{|k|}{(1+|kt|)^2}{\rm d} k\lesssim (1+t)^{-1}\int_{|k|\leq 1}\frac{1}{1+|kt|}{\rm d} k\lesssim (1+t)^{-2}\ln(1+t)\lesssim(1+t)^{-\frac32}.
\end{align*}
Moreover, it follows from the Poincaré inequality and Proposition \ref{ap3.2} that
\begin{equation}\notag
\begin{aligned}
&\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\mathcal{M}_2u\|_{L^2L^2}\\
\leq&\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\mathcal{M}_2u\|_{L^2(0,\nu^{-\frac16})L^2}+\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\mathcal{M}_2u\|_{L^2(\nu^{-\frac16},\infty)L^2}\\
\lesssim & \nu^{-\frac{1}{12}}\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{\delta}\omega\|_{L^\infty(0,\nu^{-\frac16}) L^2}+ \mathcal{E}_{in}\lesssim \mathcal{E}_{in},
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
which together with \eqref{esti u}--\eqref{uLneq} implies \eqref{1.10}. The proof of Theorem~\ref{thm1.2} is completed.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- lem-1: body_md -->
\label{l3.1}
Suppose that $(\omega_{a,k},\theta_{a,k})$ solves system \eqref{3.2} and $V_1$ satisfies \eqref{2.2}--\eqref{2.3}. Then for $k \in \mathbb{Z}$ and $ k\neq 0$, $t \geq 0$, $j = 0,1,2,3$, it holds that
\begin{align}
&\|(k, \partial_y)^j ({\rm e}^{ikyt} \theta_{a,k})(t)\|_{L^2_y} \lesssim |k|^{j-3} {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} |k|^{\frac{2}{3}} t} E_{2}(k),\label{3.3}\\
&\|(k, \partial_y)^j ({\rm e}^{ikyt} \omega_{a,k})(t)\|_{L^2_y}\lesssim |k|^{j-3}{\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} |k|^{\frac{2}{3}} t} \left(E_{1}(k)+ \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}E_{2}(k)\right).\label{3.4}
\end{align}
For $k \in \mathbb{Z}$ and $ k\neq 0$, $t \geq 0$, $j = 0,1,2$, there holds
\begin{align}
&\|(k, \partial_y)^j ({\rm e}^{ikyt} \theta_{a,k})(t)\|_{L^\infty_y} \lesssim |k|^{j-\frac{5}{2}} {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} |k|^{\frac{2}{3}} t} E_{2}(k),\label{3.5}\\
&\|(k, \partial_y)^j ({\rm e}^{ikyt} \omega_{a,k})(t)\|_{L^\infty_y} \lesssim |k|^{j - \frac{5}{2}} {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} |k|^{\frac{2}{3}} t}\left(E_{1}(k)+ \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}E_{2}(k)\right).\label{3.6}
\end{align}
<!-- end lem-1 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
By direct calculation, $\theta_{a,k}$ is given by
\begin{equation}\label{3.7}
\theta_{a,k}(t,y) = \Theta_{in,k}(y) {\rm e}^{-ikV_1^{\star}(t,y) - \nu k^2 t^3/3}, 
\end{equation}
where $ V_1^{\star}(t,y) = \int_0^t V_1(\tau,y)\,{\rm d} \tau$.
By applying \eqref{2.2}, we have
\begin{equation}\label{3.8}
\|V_1^{\star} - ty\|_{C^3} \lesssim t \|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3} \leq \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t.
\end{equation}
With the expression of $\theta_{a,k}$, by solving non-homogeneous ordinary differential equation \eqref{3.2}$_2$, we can get
\begin{align}\label{3.9}
{\rm e}^{ikyt} \omega_{a,k}(t,y) =& \exp\!\left( -\frac{\nu k^2 t^3}{3} - ik (V_1^{\star}(t,y) - yt)\right) \notag\\
&\cdot\left(\Omega_{in,k}(y) - ik\int_0^t \exp\!\left( \frac{\nu k^2s^3}{3}+ik(V_1^{\star}(s,y) - ys)\right) {\rm e}^{ikys} \theta_{a,k}(s,y){\rm d} \tau\right) \notag\\
=&\exp\!\left( -\frac{\nu k^2 t^3}{3} - ik (V_1^{\star}(t,y) - yt)\right)\left(\Omega_{in,k}(y) - ikt\Theta_{in,k}(y)\right).
\end{align}
Let $h(t) = -k(V_1^{\star}(t,y) - ty)$. Thanks to \eqref{3.8}, we deduce $|\partial_y^j h| \lesssim \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} |kt|$ and $|\partial_y^j({\rm e}^{ih})| \lesssim(1+\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} |kt|)^j$ for $j = 0,1,2,3$, then it holds that
\begin{align*}
|\partial_y^j({\rm e}^{ih - \nu k^2 t^3/3})| &\lesssim (1 + \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} |kt|)^j {\rm e}^{-\nu k^2 t^3/3} \lesssim |k|^{\frac{j}{3}}(1 + \nu k^2 t^3)^{\frac{j}{3}} {\rm e}^{-\nu k^2 t^3/3} \\
&\lesssim |k|^{\frac{j}{3}} {\rm e}^{-3\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} |k|^{\frac{2}{3}} t},
\end{align*}
\begin{equation}\label{3.10}
   |(k, \partial_y)^j({\rm e}^{ih - \nu k^2 t^3/3})| \lesssim |k|^j {\rm e}^{-3\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} |k|^{\frac{2}{3}} t}. 
\end{equation}
Thus, for $j = 0,1,2,3$, we deduce that
\begin{align}
\|(k, \partial_y)^j ({\rm e}^{ikyt} \theta_{a,k})\|_{L^2_y} &= \|(k, \partial_y)^j \big(\Theta_{in,k} {\rm e}^{ih - \nu k^2 t^3/3}\big)\|_{L^2_y} \notag\\
&\lesssim \sum_{l=0}^j \|(k, \partial_y)^l (\Theta_{in,k}(y))\|_{L^2_y} \|(k, \partial_y)^{j-l} ({\rm e}^{ih - \nu k^2 t^3/3})\|_{L^\infty_y} \notag\\
&\lesssim \sum_{l=0}^j |k|^{l-3}E_{2}(k)  |k|^{j-l} {\rm e}^{-3\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} |k|^{\frac{2}{3}} t} \notag\\
&\lesssim |k|^{j-3} {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} |k|^{\frac{2}{3}} t}E_{2}(k).\notag
\end{align}
In a similar way, we derive
\begin{align*}
\|(k, \partial_y)^j ({\rm e}^{ikyt} \omega_{a,k})\|_{L^2_y} 
\lesssim& |k|^{j-3}{\rm e}^{-3\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} |k|^{\frac{2}{3}} t}(E_{1}(k)+tE_{2}(k))\\
\lesssim& |k|^{j-3}{\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} |k|^{\frac{2}{3}} t}(E_{1}(k)+ \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}E_{2}(k)).
\end{align*}
For $j = 0,1,2$, there holds
\begin{align*}
\|(k, \partial_y)^j ({\rm e}^{ikyt} \theta_{a,k})\|_{L^\infty_y} &\lesssim \|(k, \partial_y)^j ({\rm e}^{ikyt} \theta_{a,k})\|_{L^2_y}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(k, \partial_y)^{j+1} ({\rm e}^{ikyt} \theta_{a,k})\|_{L^2_y}^{\frac{1}{2}}  \\
&\lesssim |k|^{j - \frac{5}{2}} {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} |k|^{\frac{2}{3}} t}E_{2}(k),
\end{align*}
\begin{align*}
\|(k, \partial_y)^j ({\rm e}^{ikyt} \omega_{a,k})\|_{L^\infty_y} &\lesssim \|(k, \partial_y)^j ({\rm e}^{ikyt} \omega_{a,k})\|_{L^2_y}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(k, \partial_y)^{j+1} ({\rm e}^{ikyt} \omega_{a,k})\|_{L^2_y}^{\frac{1}{2}}  \\
&\lesssim |k|^{j - \frac{5}{2}}{\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} |k|^{\frac{2}{3}} t} \left(E_{1}(k)+ \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}E_{2}(k)\right).
\end{align*}
The proof of Lemma \ref{l3.1} is completed.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- lem-2: body_md -->
\label{l3.2}
  Suppose that $(\omega_{a},\theta_{a})$ solves system \eqref{2.7} and $V_1$ satisfies \eqref{2.2}--\eqref{2.3}, then we have
   \begin{align}
      &t \|\partial_x \theta_{a}(t)\|_{L^\infty_y}+t \|\partial_x^2 \theta_{a}(t)\|_{L^\infty_y}  \lesssim \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} {\rm e}^{-\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4},\notag \\
      &t \|\partial_x \omega_{a}(t)\|_{L^\infty_y}+t \|\partial_x^2 \omega_{a}(t)\|_{L^\infty_y}  \lesssim \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} {\rm e}^{-\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}).\notag
   \end{align}
<!-- end lem-2 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
The proof of this lemma is similar to Lemma 3.3 in \cite{wei-zhang2026}, and is therefore omitted.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- lem-3: body_md -->
\label{l3.3}
If $k \in \mathbb{Z}$ and $k \neq 0$, $\omega \in H^3(-1,1)$, $\phi = -\Delta_k^{-1} \omega$, $\omega(\pm1)=0$. Then it holds for $t \geq 0$ that
\[
k^2 \|(k, \partial_y) \phi(t)\|_{L^2_y} \lesssim (1+t)^{-1} \|(k, \partial_y)({\rm e}^{ikyt} \omega)(t)\|_{L^2_y},
\]
\[
|k|^4 \|\phi(t)\|_{L^2_y} \lesssim (1+t)^{-2} \|(k, \partial_y)^2 ({\rm e}^{ikyt} \omega)(t)\|_{L^2_y},
\]
\[
|k|^2 \|(k, \partial_y)(\partial_y \phi + ikt \phi)(t)\|_{L^2_y} \lesssim (1+t)^{-1} \|(k, \partial_y)^2 ({\rm e}^{ikyt} \omega)(t)\|_{L^2_y},
\]
\[
|k|^4 \|(\partial_y \phi + ikt \phi)(t)\|_{L^2_y} \lesssim (1+t)^{-2} \|(k, \partial_y)^3 ({\rm e}^{ikyt} \omega)(t)\|_{L^2_y}.
\]
<!-- end lem-3 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
For $(\theta_{a},\omega_{a})$,
due to the fact that
\[
\theta_{a}(t,x,y) = \sum_{k \neq 0} \theta_{a,k}(t,y) {\rm e}^{ikx}, \quad
\partial_x \theta_{a}(t,x,y) = \sum_{k \neq 0} ik \theta_{a,k}(t,y) {\rm e}^{ikx},
\]
\[
(\partial_y + t\partial_x)\theta_{a}(t,x,y) = \sum_{k \neq 0} (\partial_y + ikt) \theta_{a,k}(t,y) {\rm e}^{ikx}
= \sum_{k \neq 0} \partial_y({\rm e}^{ikyt} \theta_{a,k})(t,y) {\rm e}^{ik(x - yt)}.
\]
Then it follows from \eqref{aaa3.1} and Lemma~\ref{l3.1} that
\begin{align}\label{3.12}
&\|\partial_x \theta_{a}(t)\|_{L^\infty} + \|(\partial_y + t\partial_x)\theta_{a}(t)\|_{L^\infty}\notag\\
\lesssim&  \sum_{k \neq 0} \|(k, \partial_y)({\rm e}^{ikyt} \theta_{a,k})(t)\|_{L^\infty_y}
\lesssim \sum_{k \neq 0} |k|^{-\frac{3}{2}} E_{2}(k) {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} |k|^{\frac{2}{3}} t}\notag\\
\lesssim&  \left( \sum_{k \neq 0 } |k|^{-3}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left( \sum_{k \neq 0 } (E_{2}(k))^2 \right)^{\frac{1}{2}} {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t}
\lesssim {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}.
\end{align}
Similarly, we can also prove that
\begin{align}\label{3.13}
&\|\partial_x \omega_{a}(t)\|_{L^\infty} + \|(\partial_y + t\partial_x)\omega_{a}(t)\|_{L^\infty}\notag\\
\lesssim&  \sum_{k \neq 0 } \|(k, \partial_y)({\rm e}^{ikyt} \omega_{a,k})(t)\|_{L^\infty_y}
\lesssim \sum_{k \neq 0} |k|^{-\frac{3}{2}} {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} |k|^{\frac{2}{3}} t}(E_{1}(k)+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}E_{2}(k))\notag\\
\lesssim&{\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}).
\end{align}
Thus, we obtain \eqref{2.10}--\eqref{2.11}.

 For \eqref{2.12}--\eqref{2.13}, the proof is similar to that of (2.6)--(2.7) in \cite{wei-zhang2026}, here we omit the details.

Finally,
note that
\begin{align}
&Er_{\theta}^2 = \partial_t \theta_{a} - \nu \Delta \theta_{a} + V_1 \partial_x \theta_{a}, \quad Er^2 = Er_{\theta}^2 -V_2' \partial_x \phi_{a} + u_{a} \cdot \nabla \theta_{a},\notag\\ 
&Er_{\omega}^1 = \partial_t \omega_{a}  + V_1 \partial_x \omega_{a}- \nu \Delta \omega_{a}+\partial_x\theta_{a}, \quad Er^1 = Er_{\omega}^1 - V_1'' \partial_x \phi_{a} + u_{a} \cdot \nabla \omega_{a}.\notag
\end{align}
From $\partial_x \phi_{a} = -u_{a}^2$, \eqref{2.4} and \eqref{2.12}, we derive
\[
\|V_2'\partial_x \phi_{a}(t)\| \leq \|V_2'\|_{L^\infty} \|u_{a}^2(t)\| \lesssim(1+t)^{-2} {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^3} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}).
\]
Moreover, 
\[
u_{a} \cdot \nabla \theta_{a} = u_{a}^1 \partial_x \theta_{a} + u_{a}^2 \partial_y \theta_{a} = (u_{a}^1 - t u_{a}^2) \partial_x \theta_{a} + u_{a}^2 (\partial_y + t \partial_x) \theta_{a}.
\]
Then it follows from \eqref{2.10} and \eqref{2.12} that
\begin{align*}
\|u_{a} \cdot \nabla \theta_{a}(t)\| &\leq \|(u_{a}^1 - t u_{a}^2)(t)\| \|\partial_x \theta_{a}(t)\|_{L^\infty} + \|u_{a}^2(t)\| \|(\partial_y + t \partial_x)\theta_{a}(t)\|_{L^\infty} \\
&\lesssim(1+t)^{-2} {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}(\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}).
\end{align*}
Notice that $(\partial_t + V_1 \partial_x- \nu t^2 \partial_x^2 )\theta_{a} = 0$ and $Er^2_{\theta} = (\partial_t + V_1 \partial_x- \nu \Delta)\theta_{a}$, we obtain
\[
Er^2_{\theta} = (\nu t^2 \partial_x^2 - \nu \Delta)\theta_{a}= \sum_{k \neq 0} \bar\Theta_k(t,y) {\rm e}^{ikx},
\]
here
\[
\bar\Theta_k(t,y) = -\nu (\partial_y^2 - k^2 + k^2 t^2) \theta_{a,k}(t,y).
\]
Thus, we arrive at
\[
{\rm e}^{ikyt} \bar\Theta_k = -\nu \left((\partial_y - ikt)^2 - k^2 + k^2 t^2\right)({\rm e}^{ikyt} \theta_{a,k})
= -\nu (\partial_y^2 - 2ikt \partial_y - k^2)({\rm e}^{ikyt} \theta_{a,k}).
\]
We use Lemma~\ref{l3.1} and \eqref{aaa3.1} to obtain 
\begin{align*}
\|\bar\Theta_k(t)\|_{L^2_y} &=\|{\rm e}^{ikyt}\bar\Theta_k(t)\|_{L^2_y}= \nu\| (\partial_y^2 - 2ikt \partial_y - k^2)({\rm e}^{ikyt} \theta_{a,k})(t)\|_{L^2_y} \\
&\lesssim \nu (1+t) \|(k, \partial_y)^2 ({\rm e}^{ikyt} \theta_{a,k})(t)\|_{L^2_y} \\
&\lesssim \nu (1+t) |k|^{-1} {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} |k|^{\frac{2}{3}} t} E_{2}(k) \lesssim \nu (1+t) {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} E_{2}(k),
\end{align*}
and then
\begin{align*}
\|Er^2_{\theta}(t)\|^2 &\lesssim \sum_{k \neq 0} \|\bar\Theta_k(t)\|_{L^2_y}^2\lesssim \sum_{k \neq 0} \nu^2 (1+t)^2 {\rm e}^{-4\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} (E_{2}(k))^2 \\
&\lesssim \nu^2 (1+t)^2 {\rm e}^{-4\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}^2.
\end{align*}
Summing up, there holds
\begin{align*}
\|Er^2(t)\| &\leq \|Er^2_{\theta}(t)\| + \|V_2' \partial_x \phi_{a}(t)\| + \|u_{a} \cdot \nabla \theta_{a}(t)\| \\
&\lesssim \nu (1+t) {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} +(1+t)^{-2} {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}(\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}).
\end{align*}
Similarly, we can also prove \eqref{2.14}, and then finish the proof of Proposition \ref{p2.1}.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- lem-4: body_md -->
\label{al3.3}
For $k \in \mathbb{R} \setminus \{0\}$, $t \geq 0$, $j = 0,1,2,3$, it holds that
\begin{align}
&\|(k, \partial_y)^j ({\rm e}^{ikyt} \theta_{a,k})(t)\|_{L^2_y} \lesssim {\rm e}^{-\nu t- \nu k^2 t^3/4}E_{2}(k),\label{a3.25}\\
&\|(k, \partial_y)^j ({\rm e}^{ikyt} \omega_{a,k})(t)\|_{L^2_y}\lesssim {\rm e}^{-\nu t- \nu k^2 t^3/4}\left(E_{1}(k)+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}E_{2}(k)\right).\label{a3.26}
\end{align}
For $k \in \mathbb{R} \setminus \{0\}$, $t \geq 0$, $j = 0,1,2$, it holds that
\begin{align}
&\|(k, \partial_y)^j ({\rm e}^{ikyt} \theta_{a,k})(t)\|_{L^\infty_y} \lesssim {\rm e}^{-\nu t- \nu k^2 t^3/4}E_{2}(k),\label{a3.27}\\
&\|(k, \partial_y)^j ({\rm e}^{ikyt} \omega_{a,k})(t)\|_{L^\infty_y} \lesssim {\rm e}^{-\nu t- \nu k^2 t^3/4}\left(E_{1}(k)+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}E_{2}(k)\right).\label{a3.28}
\end{align}
<!-- end lem-4 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
According to \eqref{a3.24}$_1$, we know that 
\begin{equation}\label{a3.29}
\theta_{a,k}(t,y) = \Theta_{in,k}(y) {\rm e}^{-\nu t-ikyt - \nu k^2 t^3/3}. 
\end{equation}
Then, we can deduce 
\begin{align}\label{a3.30}
{\rm e}^{ikyt} \omega_{a,k}(t,y) =& \exp\!\left( -\nu t-\frac{\nu k^2 t^3}{3} \right)\left(\Omega_{in,k}(y) - ik\int_0^t \exp\!\left(\nu s+ \frac{\nu k^2s^3}{3}\right) {\rm e}^{ikys} \theta_{a,k}(s,y){\rm d} s\right) \notag\\
=&\exp\!\left(-\nu t -\frac{\nu k^2 t^3}{3} \right)\left(\Omega_{in,k}(y) - ikt\Theta_{in,k}(y)\right).
\end{align}
Thus, similar to Lemma \ref{l3.1}, we can obtain \eqref{a3.25}--\eqref{a3.28}. 
 This completes the proof of this lemma.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- lem-5: body_md -->
\label{al3.4}
Assume that $k \in \mathbb{R} \setminus \{0\}$, $\omega \in H^3(-1,1)$, $\phi = -\Delta_k^{-1} \omega$, $\omega(\pm1)=0$. Then it holds that for $|k|\geq 1$,
\begin{align}
|k|^2\|(k, \partial_y)\phi\|_{L^2_y}\lesssim& (1+t)^{-1}\|(k, \partial_y) ({\rm e}^{ik yt} \omega)\|_{L^2_y},, \label{psiL2k>1}\\
|k|^2 \|(k, \partial_y)(\partial_y \phi+ik t \phi)\|_{L^2_y}\lesssim&(1+t)^{-1}\|(k, \partial_y)^2 ({\rm e}^{ik yt} \omega)\|_{L^2_y}, \label{paypay+kpsiL2}\\
|k|^4\|\phi\|_{L^2_y}\lesssim&(1+t)^{-2}\|(k, \partial_y)^2 ({\rm e}^{ik yt} \omega)\|_{L^2_y},\label{k>1, psiL2}\\
| k|^4\|(\partial_y+i k t)\phi\|_{L^2_y}\lesssim&(1+t)^{-2}\|(k, \partial_y)^3 ({\rm e}^{ik yt} \omega)\|_{L^2_y},\label{xi>1 xi4pay+xitpsiL2}
\end{align}
and for $|k|< 1$,
\begin{align}
\|(1, \partial_y)\phi\|_{L^2_y}\lesssim& (1+|kt|)^{-1}\|(1, \partial_y) ({\rm e}^{ik yt} \omega)\|_{L^2_y},  \label{psiL2k<1}\\
\|(1, \partial_y)(\partial_y\phi+ik t\phi)\|_{L^2_y}\lesssim&(1+|kt|)^{-1}\|(1, \partial_y)^2 ({\rm e}^{ik yt} \omega)\|_{L^2_y},\label{esti3 paypaypsi+xiytpsi}\\
\|\phi\|_{L^2_y}\lesssim&(1+|kt|)^{-2}\|(1, \partial_y)^2 ({\rm e}^{ik yt} \omega)\|_{L^2_y}, \label{esti4 psiL2}\\
\|(\partial_y+i k t)\phi\|_{L^2_y}\lesssim&(1+| k t|)^{-2}\|(1, \partial_y)^3 ({\rm e}^{ik yt} \omega)\|_{L^2_y}. \label{xi<1 pay+xitpsiL2}
\end{align}
<!-- end lem-5 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
For details of the proof, see Lemma A.4 in  \cite{miao2025}, we omit the proof here.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
We only prove \eqref{u1LLinfty}--\eqref{u12L22}, and the proofs of other formulas are similar to those in \cite{miao2025}. 
 
Define $(\widetilde{\theta},\widetilde{\omega})(t,x,y)=(\theta_{a},\omega_{a})(t,x+ty,y)$. We have from \eqref{a3.24} that
\begin{equation}\notag
\left\{
\begin{aligned}
&\partial_t \tilde{\theta} + \nu (1-t^2 \partial_x^2) \tilde{\theta}  = 0,\\
&\partial_t \tilde{\omega} + \nu (1-t^2 \partial_x^2) \tilde{\omega}  = -\partial_x\tilde{\theta},\ \  x\in \mathbb{R},\ y\in I, \\
&\omega_{a}|_{t=0} = \Omega_{in},\ \theta_{a}|_{t=0} = \Theta_{in},\\ 
&\tilde{\omega}(t,x\mp t,\pm1)=\tilde{\theta}(t,x\mp t,\pm1)=0.
\end{aligned}
\right.
\end{equation}
Then we can solve the above system as 
\begin{equation}\notag
\tilde{\theta} = {\rm e}^{-\nu t} {\rm e}^{\frac{\nu t^3}{3}\partial_x^2} \Theta_{in}, \ \ \ \tilde{\omega} = {\rm e}^{-\nu t} {\rm e}^{\frac{\nu t^3}{3}\partial_x^2} \Omega_{in} - \int_0^t {\rm e}^{-\nu(t-s)} \partial_x {\rm e}^{A(t,s)\partial_x^2}  \tilde{\theta}(s){\rm d} s,
\end{equation}
where $A(t, s) = \nu \int_s^t \tau^2 {\rm d}\tau = \frac{\nu}{3} (t^3 - s^3).$ Using $\mathcal{M}_1 \leq 3|k|^{\frac{2}{3}}$ and the heat kernel estimate, we obtain
\begin{align}\label{omLxy}
\| (1 + \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t \mathcal{M}_1)^{m} \tilde{\theta} \|_{L_x^1 L_y^\infty} 
\lesssim_m {\rm e}^{-\nu t} \| \Theta_{in} \|_{L_x^1 L_y^\infty},
\end{align}
and then by $1 + \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t \mathcal{M}_1 \leq (1 + \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} s \mathcal{M}_1)(1 + \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} (t-s) \mathcal{M}_1)$ and $\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} (t-s)\leq (\nu(t^3-s^3))^{\frac{1}{3}}$,
\begin{align}\label{omLxy1}
\| (1 + \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t \mathcal{M}_1)^{m} \tilde{\omega} \|_{L_x^1 L_y^\infty} 
\lesssim_m& {\rm e}^{-\nu t} \| \Omega_{in} \|_{L_x^1 L_y^\infty}+ \int_0^t {\rm e}^{-\nu(t-s)} A(t,s)^{-\frac{1}{2}} \|(1 + \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} s \mathcal{M}_1)^{m}\tilde{\theta}(s)\|_{L_x^1 L_y^\infty} {\rm d} s\notag\\
\lesssim_m&{\rm e}^{-\nu t} \| \Omega_{in} \|_{L_x^1 L_y^\infty}+ \nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\rm e}^{-\nu t}\| \Theta_{in} \|_{L_x^1 L_y^\infty}\int_0^t (t^3 - s^3)^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\rm d} s\notag\\
\lesssim_m&{\rm e}^{-\nu t} \| \Omega_{in} \|_{L_x^1 L_y^\infty}+ \nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\rm e}^{-\nu t}\| \Theta_{in} \|_{L_x^1 L_y^\infty}.
\end{align}
Define $(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}\phi_{a,1}$ be the solution to $\partial^2_y (1+\nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m} \phi_{a,1}=(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}\omega_{a}$ with $\phi_{a,1}(\pm1)=0.$ 
By \eqref{omLxy}--\eqref{omLxy1}, we can deduce that 
\begin{align}\label{a3.43}
&\|\partial_y(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}\phi_{a,1}\|_{L^\infty_y}\leq \|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}\omega_{a}\|_{L^1_y}\notag\\ 
&= \int^{1}_{-1}|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}(\widetilde{\omega}(t,x-ty,y))|{\rm d}y\lesssim_mt^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|(1+\nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}\widetilde{\omega}(t,s,y)|{\rm d}s\notag\\
 &\lesssim_m t^{-1}{\rm e}^{-\nu t}(\| \Omega_{in} \|_{L_x^1 L_y^\infty}+ \nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\rm e}^{-\nu t}\| \Theta_{in} \|_{L_x^1 L_y^\infty}).
\end{align}
Moreover, it follows from $\Delta \phi_{a}=\omega_{a}$ that
\begin{align*}
\partial^2_y (1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}(\phi_{a,1}-\phi_{a})=(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}\partial^2_x\phi_{a}.
\end{align*}
We then apply \eqref{k>1, psiL2}, \eqref{esti4 psiL2}, \eqref{a3.26} and $\mathcal{M}_1(k)\leq 3|k|^{\frac23}$ to obtain
\begin{equation*}
\begin{aligned}
&\|\partial_y(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}(\phi_{a,1}-\phi_{a})\|_{L^\infty_y}
\leq  \|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}\partial^2_x\phi_{a}\|_{L^1_y}\\
\lesssim &\int^1_{-1}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}} (1+ \nu^{\frac13}|k|^{\frac23} t)^{m}{\rm e}^{ikx} k^2\phi_{a,k} {\rm d}k\Big|{\rm d}y\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}} (1+ \nu^{\frac13}|k|^{\frac23} t)^{m}|k|^2\|\phi_{a,k}\|_{L^2_y}{\rm d}k\\
\lesssim_m&\int_{|k|\leq 1} \frac{|k|^2{\rm e}^{-\nu t}}{(1+kt)^2}(E_1+\nu^{-\frac13}E_2){\rm d}k+\int_{|k|\geq 1}(1+t)^{-2}{\rm e}^{-\nu t}|k|^{-2}(E_1+\nu^{-\frac13}E_2){\rm d}k\\
\lesssim_m&(1+t)^{-2}{\rm e}^{-\nu t}(\|E_1(k)\|_{L^2_k}+\nu^{-\frac13}\|E_2(k)\|_{L^2_k})\lesssim_m(1+t)^{-2}{\rm e}^{-\nu t}\mathcal{E}_{in},
\end{aligned}
\end{equation*}
which together with \eqref{a3.43} implies
\begin{align}\label{payphiLinfty}
\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}\partial_y \phi_{a}\|_{L^\infty_{x,y}}\lesssim_m&t^{-1}{\rm e}^{-\nu t}(\mathcal{E}_{in}+\| \Omega_{in} \|_{L_x^1 L_y^\infty}+ \nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\| \Theta_{in} \|_{L_x^1 L_y^\infty})\notag\\ 
\lesssim_m&t^{-1}{\rm e}^{-\nu t}(\mathcal{E}_{in}+t^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{E}_{in}).
\end{align}
In addition, using \eqref{psiL2k>1}, \eqref{paypay+kpsiL2}, \eqref{psiL2k<1}, \eqref{esti3 paypaypsi+xiytpsi} and \eqref{a3.26}, we deduce
\begin{align*}
\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}\partial_y\phi_{a}\|_{L^\infty_{x,y}}\lesssim & \int_{\mathbb{R}} (1+ \nu^{\frac13}|k|^{\frac23} t)^{m}\|{\rm e}^{ikty}\partial_y\phi_{a,k}\|_{L^\infty_y}{\rm d}k\\
\lesssim &  \int_{\mathbb{R}} (1+ \nu^{\frac13}|k|^{\frac23} t)^{m}\|\partial_y(\partial_y+ikt)\phi_{a,k}\|^{\frac12}_{L^2_y}\|\partial_y\phi_{a,k}\|^{\frac12}_{L^2_y}{\rm d}k\\
\lesssim_m&\int_{|k|\leq 1}{\rm e}^{-\nu t} (E_1+\nu^{-\frac13}E_2) {\rm d}k+\int_{|k|\geq 1} {\rm e}^{-\nu t}|k|^{-2}(E_1+\nu^{-\frac13}E_2) {\rm d}k\\ 
\lesssim_m&{\rm e}^{-\nu t}(\|E_1(k)\|_{L^2_k}+\nu^{-\frac13}\|E_2(k)\|_{L^2_k})\lesssim_m {\rm e}^{-\nu t}\mathcal{E}_{in},
\end{align*}
which together with \eqref{payphiLinfty} leads to
\begin{align}\label{payphi}
\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}\partial_y \phi_{a}\|_{L^\infty}\lesssim_m(1+t)^{-1}{\rm e}^{-\nu t}\mathcal{E}_{in}.
\end{align}
Similarly, by taking $\partial^2_y (1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m} (\partial_y+t\partial_x)\phi_{a,1}=(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}(\partial_y+t\partial_x)\omega_{a}$, we can obtain the estimate of $\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}(\partial_y+t\partial_x)\phi_{a}\|_{L^\infty}$.
Next, 
\begin{align*}
\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}\partial_x\phi_{a}\|_{L^\infty_y}
\lesssim& \int_{\mathbb{R}} (1+ \nu^{\frac13}|k|^{\frac23} t)^{m}|k|\|{\rm e}^{ikty}\phi_{a,k}\|_{L^\infty_y}{\rm d}k\\
\lesssim& \int_{\mathbb{R}} (1+ \nu^{\frac13}|k|^{\frac23} t)^{m}|k|\|(1,\partial_y)({\rm e}^{ikty}\phi_{a,k})\|_{L^2_y}{\rm d}k,
\end{align*}
then applying \eqref{xi>1 xi4pay+xitpsiL2}, \eqref{xi<1 pay+xitpsiL2} and \eqref{a3.26} gives
\begin{align*}
 &\int_{\mathbb{R}} (1+ \nu^{\frac13}|k|^{\frac23} t)^{m}|k|\|(1,\partial_y)({\rm e}^{ikty}\phi_{a,k})\|_{L^2_y}{\rm d}k\\
\lesssim_m&{\rm e}^{-\nu t}\Big(\int_{|k|\leq 1} \frac{|k|}{(1+|kt|)^2}(E_1+\nu^{-\frac13}E_2){\rm d}k+\int_{|k|\geq 1}(1+t)^{-2}|k|^{-3}(E_1+\nu^{-\frac13}E_2){\rm d}k\Big)\\
\lesssim_m&(1+t)^{-2}\ln(1+t){\rm e}^{-\nu t}\mathcal{E}_{in},
\end{align*}
which implies
\begin{align}\notag
\|(1+ \nu^{\frac13}t\mathcal{M}_1)^{m}\partial_x\phi_{a}\|_{L^\infty}\lesssim_m(1+t)^{-2}\ln(1+t){\rm e}^{-\nu t}\mathcal{E}_{in}.
\end{align}
Thus, one gets \eqref{u1LLinfty}--\eqref{u12L22}.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- prop-5: body_md -->
\label{p4.1}
Suppose that $V_1(t,y)$ satisfies \eqref{eq:4.1}, $u_{a}$ is defined as in \eqref{2.7}--\eqref{2.8} and $f$ solves
\begin{equation}\label{4.2}
\left\{
\begin{aligned}
&\partial_t f - \nu \Delta f + V_1 \partial_x f+u_{a}\cdot\nabla f = 0, \ \ \ \  t>0,\\
&f|_{t=0}=f_{in}(x,y),\quad f(t,x,\pm 1) = 0, \quad \nabla\cdot u_{a}=0,
\end{aligned}
\right.
\end{equation}
then for $0<t\leq \nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}$, we have
\begin{align}
\|\partial_x f(t)\| &\lesssim (\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}} (1+t)^{-1} \|f_{in}\|, \label{aa1}\\
\|f_{\ne}(t)\| &\lesssim (1 + \nu t^3)^{-\frac{1}{2}} \|f_{in}\|.\label{aa2}
\end{align}
<!-- end prop-5 -->
<!-- lem-6: body_md -->
\label{lem:4.1}
Under the hypothesis of the Proposition \ref{p4.1}, we have
\begin{align}
&\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t} \|f\|^2+2\nu \|\nabla f\|^2=0, \label{4.4}\\
&\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t} \|\nabla f\|^2 + 2\nu \|\Delta f\|^2 \leq 3 \|\partial_xf\| \|\partial_y f\|+C\varepsilon_0\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\|\nabla f\|^2, \label{4.5}\\
&\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t} \langle \partial_xf, \partial_y f \rangle + \|\sqrt{V_1'+\partial_yu_{a}^1} \partial_xf\|^2 \leq 2\nu \|\partial_{xy} f\| \|\Delta f\|+C\varepsilon_0\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}(1+t)^{-2}\|\partial_yf\|^2.\label{4.6}
\end{align}
<!-- end lem-6 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
Firstly, by taking the inner product of both sides of \eqref{4.2}$_1$ with $f$ and using $\nabla \cdot u_{a}=0$, we obtain \eqref{4.4}.

Next, taking the inner product of \eqref{4.2}$_1$ with $\Delta f$ yields
\begin{align}
\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t} \|\nabla f\|^2 &= -2 \langle \Delta f, \partial_t f \rangle = -2\nu \|\Delta f\|^2 + 2  \langle \Delta f, (V_1+u_{a}^1) \partial_xf+u_{a}^2\partial_yf \rangle.\notag
\end{align}
It can be directly calculated that
\begin{align}
\langle \Delta f,  V_1 \partial_xf \rangle =&  \langle \partial_{xx} f,  V_1\partial_xf \rangle -  \langle \partial_y f, \partial_y (V_1 \partial_xf) \rangle = - \langle \partial_y f,  V_1'\partial_xf  \rangle\notag\\ 
\leq&\|V_1'\|_{L^\infty}\|\partial_xf\| \|\partial_y f\|\leq \frac{5}{4}\|\partial_xf\| \|\partial_y f\|,\notag
\end{align}
\begin{align}
&\langle \Delta f,  u_{a}^1 \partial_xf+u_{a}^2\partial_yf \rangle= \langle \partial_{xx} f+\partial_{yy} f, u_{a}^1 \partial_xf+u_{a}^2\partial_yf \rangle \notag\\ 
=&-\frac{1}{2}\langle |\partial_{x} f|^2, \partial_{x}u_{a}^1\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\langle |\partial_{y} f|^2,  \partial_{y}u_{a}^2\rangle-\langle \partial_{x} f,\partial_{x}u_{a}^2\partial_yf \rangle-\langle \partial_{x} f,u_{a}^2\partial_{xy}f \rangle\notag\\ 
&-\langle \partial_{y} f,\partial_{y}u_{a}^1\partial_xf \rangle- \langle \partial_{y} f,u_{a}^1\partial_{xy}f \rangle  \notag\\
=&\frac{1}{2}\langle |\partial_{y} f|^2-|\partial_{x} f|^2, \partial_{x}u_{a}^1-\partial_{y}u_{a}^2\rangle-\langle \partial_{x} f,\partial_{x}u_{a}^2\partial_yf \rangle-\langle \partial_{y} f,\partial_{y}u_{a}^1\partial_xf \rangle\notag\\
\leq& \|\nabla u_{a}\|_{L^\infty}(\|\partial_xf\|^2+\|\partial_yf\|^2)+2\|\nabla u_{a}\|_{L^\infty}\|\partial_xf\| \|\partial_y f\|\notag\\ 
\leq& \frac{1}{4}\|\partial_xf\| \|\partial_y f\|+C\varepsilon_0\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\|\nabla f\|^2.\notag
\end{align}
Here we used
\[
\|V_1'\|_{L^\infty} \leq 1 + \|V_1' - 1\|_{L^\infty} \leq 1 + C\varepsilon_0 < \frac{5}{4},\ \ \|\nabla u_{a}\|_{L^\infty}\leq  C\varepsilon_0\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}< \frac{1}{4}.
\]
Combining the above four formulas, we can get \eqref{4.5}.

At last, by direct calculation, we have
\begin{align}
\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t} \langle \partial_xf, \partial_y f \rangle =& \langle \partial_x (\nu \Delta f -  (V_1+u_{a}^1) \partial_xf-u_{a}^2\partial_y f), \partial_y f \rangle  \notag\\
&+ \langle \partial_xf, \partial_y (\nu \Delta f -(V_1+u_{a}^1) \partial_xf-u_{a}^2\partial_y f ) \rangle \notag\\
=&-\|\sqrt{V_1'+\partial_yu_{a}^1} \partial_xf\|^2 +\nu \langle   \partial_x\Delta f, \partial_y f \rangle +\nu \langle \partial_xf,  \partial_y \Delta f \rangle\notag\\ 
&- \langle \partial_x (u_{a}^2\partial_y f), \partial_y f \rangle -  \langle \partial_xf, \partial_y (u_{a}^2\partial_y f) \rangle. \notag
\end{align}
By using \eqref{2.12}, we have
\begin{align}
|\nu\langle   \partial_x\Delta f, \partial_y f \rangle +\nu \langle \partial_xf,  \partial_y \Delta f \rangle| \leq 2\nu \|\partial_{xy} f\| \|\Delta f\|,\notag
\end{align}
\begin{align}
| \langle \partial_x (u_{a}^2\partial_y f), \partial_y f \rangle +  \langle \partial_xf, \partial_y (u_{a}^2\partial_y f) \rangle|\leq& 2\|\partial_xu_{a}^2\|_{L^\infty} \|\partial_{y} f\|^2\notag\\\leq &C\varepsilon_0\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}(1+t)^{-2}\|\partial_yf\|^2.\notag
\end{align}
Combining the above three formulas implies \eqref{4.6}. Up to now, the proof of this lemma is completed.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- lem-7: body_md -->
\label{l4.4}
Under the hypothesis of Proposition \ref{p4.1}, if we take $a_1 = \frac{1}{900}$, $a_2 = 16a_1$, $a_3 = 6a_1$ and
\[
\Phi_k(t) = (1 + a_1 \nu |k|^2 t^3) \|f_k\|^2 + a_2 \nu t \|(k, \partial_y) f_k\|^2 + a_3 \nu t^2 \mathrm{Re} \langle ik f_k, \partial_y f_k \rangle,
\]
where $f$ satisfies \eqref{4.2} and $f_k$ denotes the Fourier transform of $f$ with respect to $x$, then for $0\leq t\leq \nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}$, we have
\begin{equation}\label{4.7}
  \Phi_k(t) \geq \left(1 + \frac{a_1 \nu |k|^2 t^3}{4} \right) \|f_k\|^2 + \frac{a_2}{4} \nu t \|(k, \partial_y) f_k\|^2,  
\end{equation}
\begin{equation}\label{4.8}
  \Phi_k'(t)+\frac{a_1}{2}\left[\nu |k|^2 t^2 \|f_k\|^2+\nu \|(k, \partial_y) f_k\|^2+\nu^2 t \|\Delta_k f_k\|^2+\nu^2 |k|^4 t^3 \|f_k\|^2\right]\leq 0.  
\end{equation}
<!-- end lem-7 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
Notice that $a_2 = 16a_1 > 0$, $a_3 = 6a_1$, we have
\begin{align*}
|a_3 \nu t^2 \mathrm{Re} \langle ik f_k, \partial_y f_k \rangle| &\leq \frac{a_3 }{8}\nu (t^3 |k|^2 \|f_k\|^2 + 16t \|\partial_y f_k\|^2) \\
&= \frac{3}{4}\nu (a_1 t^3 |k|^2 \|f_k\|^2 + a_2 t \|\partial_y f_k\|^2),
\end{align*}
which gives \eqref{4.7}.

Next, by applying Lemma~\ref{lem:4.1} and $|\mathrm{Re} \langle ik f_k, \partial_y f_k \rangle| \leq |k| \|f_k\| \|\partial_y f_k\|$, we can prove
\begin{align*}
\Phi_k'(t)&=3a_1 \nu |k|^2 t^2 \|f_k\|^2 + a_2 \nu  \|(k, \partial_y) f_k\|^2 + 2a_3 \nu t \mathrm{Re} \langle ik f_k, \partial_y f_k \rangle\\
&\quad+(1 + a_1 \nu |k|^2 t^3) \frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\|f_k\|^2 + a_2 \nu t \frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\|(k, \partial_y) f_k\|^2 + a_3 \nu t^2 \frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t}\mathrm{Re} \langle ik f_k, \partial_y f_k \rangle\\ 
&\leq 3a_1 \nu t^2 |k|^2 \|f_k\|^2 - 2\nu (1 + a_1 \nu |k|^2 t^3) \|(k, \partial_y) f_k\|^2 +Ca_2\varepsilon_0\nu t\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\|(k, \partial_y) f_k\|^2 \\
&\quad+ a_2 \nu \|(k, \partial_y) f_k\|^2 - 2a_2 \nu^2 t \|\Delta_k f_k\|^2 + 3a_2 \nu t |k| \|f_k\| \|\partial_y f_k\| + 2a_3 \nu t |k| \|f_k\| \|\partial_y f_k\| \\
&\quad - a_3 \nu t^2 |k|^2 \|\sqrt{V_1'+\partial_yu_{a}^1} f_k\|^2 + 2a_3 \nu^2 t^2 |k| \|\partial_y f_k\| \|\Delta_k f_k\|+Ca_3 \varepsilon_0\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\nu\|\partial_yf\|^2 \\
&= \nu |k|^2 t^2 \left(4a_1 \|f_k\|^2 - a_3 \|\sqrt{V_1'+\partial_yu_{a}^1} f_k\|^2\right) \\ 
&\quad- \nu (1 - a_2-Ca_2\varepsilon_0 t\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}-Ca_3\varepsilon_0 \nu^{\frac{1}{3}}) \|(k, \partial_y) f_k\|^2 \\
&\quad - \left[\nu |k|^2 t^2 a_1 \|f_k\|^2 - (3a_2 + 2a_3) \nu t |k| \|f_k\| \|\partial_y f_k\| + \nu \|(k, \partial_y) f_k\|^2\right] \\
&\quad - \left[2a_2 \nu^2 t \|\Delta_k f_k\|^2 - 2a_3 \nu^2 t^2 |k| \|\partial_y f_k\| \|\Delta_k f_k\| + 2a_1 \nu^2 |k|^2 t^3 \|(k, \partial_y) f_k\|^2\right] \\
&:= I_1 + I_2 + I_3 + I_4.
\end{align*}
From Proposition \ref{p2.1}, \eqref{eq:4.1} and $a_3 = 6a_1 > 0$, we have 
\[
V_1'+\partial_yu_{a}^1 \geq 1 - \|V_1' - 1\|_{L^\infty}-\|\partial_yu_{a}^1\|_{L^\infty} \geq 1 - \frac{1}{4} >\frac{3}{4}, 
\]
\begin{equation}\notag
a_3 \|\sqrt{V_1'+\partial_yu_{a}^1} f_k\|^2 \geq \frac{3a_3}{4} \|f_k\|^2 = \frac{9a_1}{2} \|f_k\|^2,
\end{equation}
this implies 
\begin{equation}\notag
 I_1\leq -\frac{a_1}{2}\nu |k|^2 t^2 \|f_k\|^2 .  
\end{equation}
For $I_2$, due to $a_2 = \frac{4}{225}$, $\varepsilon_0\ll 1$ and $t\leq \nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}$, we can deduce 
\begin{equation}\notag
    I_2\leq  -\frac{1}{2}\nu \|(k, \partial_y) f_k\|^2.
\end{equation}
Finally, note that  $a_1 = \frac{1}{900}$, $a_2 = 16a_1$, $a_3 = 6a_1$, then $3a_2 + 2a_3=60a_1 $, and
\begin{align*}
I_3 &= -[\nu |k|^2 t^2 a_1 \|f_k\|^2 - (3a_2 + 2a_3) \nu t |k| \|f_k\| \|\partial_y f_k\| + \nu \|(k, \partial_y) f_k\|^2] \\
&= -\nu a_1 (|k|t \|f_k\| - 30 \|(k, \partial_y) f_k\|)^2 \leq 0,
\end{align*}
\begin{align}
    I_4 =& -[2a_2 \nu^2 t \|\Delta_k f_k\|^2 - 2a_3 \nu^2 t^2 |k| \|\partial_y f_k\| \|\Delta_k f_k\| + 2a_1 \nu^2 |k|^2 t^3 \|(k, \partial_y) f_k\|^2]\notag\\ 
    &-[14a_1 \nu^2 t \|\Delta_k f_k\|^2 + 2a_1 \nu^2 t (3\|\Delta_k f_k\| - |k|t \|\partial_y f_k\|)^2 + 2a_1 \nu^2 |k|^4 t^3 \|f_k\|^2].\notag
\end{align}
Summing up, we arrive at \eqref{4.8}.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
According to \eqref{4.7}--\eqref{4.8}, we derive for $k\in \mathbb{Z}$ that
   \begin{align}
      \|f_{in,k}\|^2=& \Phi_k(0)\geq  \Phi_k(t) \geq \left(1 + \frac{a_1 \nu |k|^2 t^3}{4} \right) \|f_k\|^2 + \frac{a_2}{4} \nu t \|(k, \partial_y) f_k\|^2\notag\\
      \gtrsim& \nu t(1+t^2)|k|^2\|f_k\|^2.\notag
   \end{align}
  Then summing over the integer $k$, we obtain \eqref{aa1}. 

  In addition, we can also derive for $k\neq 0$,
  \begin{align}
      \|f_{in,k}\|^2=& \Phi_k(0)\geq  \Phi_k(t) \geq \left(1 + \frac{a_1 \nu |k|^2 t^3}{4} \right) \|f_k\|^2 + \frac{a_2}{4} \nu t \|(k, \partial_y) f_k\|^2\notag\\
      \gtrsim& (1+\nu |k|^2 t^3)\|f_k\|^2\gtrsim (1+\nu t^3)\|f_k\|^2.\notag
   \end{align}
   Summing over the non-zero integer $k$ gives \eqref{aa2}.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- coro-1: body_md -->
\label{c4.5}
    Under the conditions of Proposition \ref{p4.1}, if $f$ is the solution to \eqref{4.2}, then we have for $t\leq \nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}$ that
  \begin{align*}
&\|f(t)\| + \nu^{\frac{1}{2}} \left(\int_0^t \|\nabla f(\tau)\|^2 {\rm d} \tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}} + \nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_0^t \|\partial_x f(\tau)\|{\rm d} \tau+\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\int_0^t \|f_{\neq}(\tau)\|{\rm d} \tau \\
&\lesssim \|f_{in}\|.
\end{align*}
<!-- end coro-1 -->
<!-- prop-6: body_md -->
\label{p4.7}
Let $(\omega,\theta)$ be the solution to \eqref{2.9}, then for $0\leq t\leq T_0=\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}$,
\begin{align}\label{4.9}
  \|\theta\|_{X_1}\lesssim& \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}( \nu^{\frac{2}{3}}+\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4})+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}\notag\\ 
  &+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}} \|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}\|\theta\|_{X_1}+\nu^{-\frac{5}{8}} \|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}^{\frac{9}{10}}(\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2 L^2})^{\frac{1}{10}}\|\theta\|_{X_1}.  
\end{align}
<!-- end prop-6 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
Notice that
\begin{equation}\notag
    \partial_t \theta - \nu \Delta \theta + V_1 \partial_x \theta+u_{a}\cdot\nabla\theta=g_1, \ \theta|_{t=0}=0,
\end{equation}
where 
\begin{equation}\notag
    g_1=V_2' \partial_x \phi - u \cdot \nabla \theta_{a}- u \cdot \nabla \theta  -Er^2.
\end{equation}
From Corollary \ref{c4.5} and Duhamel's principle, we have 
\begin{equation}\label{4.10}
\|\theta\|_{X_1} \lesssim \left\| g_1 \right\|_{L^1 L^2}.
\end{equation}
Next, we are going to estimate $\left\| g_1 \right\|_{L^1 L^2}$.  Note that $\phi = \Delta^{-1} \omega$, using \eqref{2.4} gives
\[
\|V_2' \partial_x \phi\| \leq \|V_2' \|_{L^\infty} \|\partial_x \phi\| \lesssim \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} \|\omega\|,
\]
and then,
\begin{align*}
\|V_2' \partial_x \phi\|_{L^1 L^2} \lesssim \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} \|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}t \lesssim \nu^{-\frac{1}{6}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} \|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}.
\end{align*}
It follows from \eqref{2.10} that
\begin{align}
\|u \cdot \nabla \theta_{a}\| \lesssim \|u\| \|\nabla\theta_{a}\|_{L^\infty}\lesssim t\|\omega\|\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4},\notag
\end{align}
thus, by $0\leq t\leq \nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}$,
\begin{align}
\|u \cdot \nabla \theta_{a}\|_{L^1 L^2} \lesssim t^2\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^3}\lesssim \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}.\notag
\end{align}

Notice that
\[
u \cdot \nabla \theta = P_{\ne} u \cdot \nabla \theta + P_0 u^1 \partial_x \theta.
\]
We have from Lemma~\ref{lem:A.1} and $u = (\partial_y, -\partial_x) \Delta^{-1} \omega$ that
\begin{align*}
\|P_{\ne} u\|_{L^\infty} \lesssim \|\omega\|^{1-\frac{\delta_1}{2}} \|\nabla\omega\|^{\frac{\delta_1}{2}},\ \ \ \|P_0 u\|_{L^\infty} \lesssim \|P_0 u\|_{H^1} \lesssim \|\omega\|,
\end{align*}
and then
\begin{align*}
\|P_{\ne} u\|_{L^2 L^\infty} &\lesssim \|\omega\|_{L^2 L^2}^{1-\frac{\delta_1}{2}} \|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2 L^2}^{\frac{\delta_1}{2}} \lesssim\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}^{1-\frac{\delta_1}{2}}t^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\frac{\delta_1}{2})}\|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2 L^2}^{\frac{\delta_1}{2}}\\
&\lesssim \nu^{-\frac{1}{12}+\frac{\delta_1}{24}}\nu^{-\frac{\delta_1}{4}}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}^{1-\frac{\delta_1}{2}}(\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2 L^2})^{\frac{\delta_1}{2}}.
\end{align*}
Let $\delta_1=\frac{1}{5}$, then 
\begin{align*}
\|P_{\ne} u\|_{L^2 L^\infty} \lesssim\nu^{-\frac{1}{8}} \|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}^{\frac{9}{10}}(\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2 L^2})^{\frac{1}{10}}.
\end{align*}
Thus, by H\"older's inequality, we get
\begin{align*}
\|P_{\ne} u \cdot \nabla \theta\|_{L^1 L^2} &\leq \|P_{\ne} u\|_{L^2 L^\infty} \|\nabla \theta\|_{L^2 L^2} \lesssim \nu^{-\frac{5}{8}} \|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}^{\frac{9}{10}}(\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2 L^2})^{\frac{1}{10}}\|\theta\|_{X_1}, \\
\|P_0 u^1 \partial_x \theta\|_{L^1 L^2} &\leq \|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2} \|\partial_x \theta\|_{L^1 L^2} \lesssim \nu^{-\frac{1}{2}} \|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}\|\theta\|_{X_1}.
\end{align*}
Finally, applying \eqref{2.15}and $0 \leq t \leq \nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}$ leads to
\begin{align*}
\|Er^2\|_{L^1 L^2} &\lesssim \|\nu (1+t)\|_{L^1} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} + \|(1+t)^{-2}\|_{L^1}  \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}(\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}) \\
&\lesssim \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}(\nu^{\frac{2}{3}} +\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}).
\end{align*}

The proof of this proposition is completed.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- prop-7: body_md -->
\label{p4.8}
If $0 \leq t \leq T_0 = \nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}$, then
\begin{equation}\label{4.11}
    \|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}+\nu^{\frac{1}{2}} \|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2L^2}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\theta\|_{X_1} \lesssim \nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}.
\end{equation}
<!-- end prop-7 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
Firstly, we multiply  \eqref{2.9}$_1$ and \eqref{2.9}$_2$ by $\omega$ and $\theta$ respectively, and integrate it with respect to $x$ and $y$ to obtain
\[
\frac{1}{2} \frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t} \|\omega\|^2 + \nu \|\nabla \omega\|^2 = \int_{-1}^1\int_\mathbb{T} (V_1'' \partial_x \phi - u \cdot \nabla \omega_{a} - Er^1-\partial_x \theta) \omega {\rm d} x {\rm d} y,
\]
\[
\frac{1}{2} \frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t} \|\theta\|^2 + \nu \|\nabla \theta\|^2 = \int_{-1}^1\int_\mathbb{T} (V_2' \partial_x \phi -u \cdot \nabla \theta_{a} - Er^2) \theta {\rm d} x {\rm d} y.
\]
Using $u = (\partial_y, -\partial_x) \phi$ and $\phi = \Delta^{-1} \omega$, we have $\|u\| = \|\nabla \phi\| \leq C \|\omega\|$,
and
\begin{align*}
&\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t} \|\omega\|^2_{L^2}+\nu \|\nabla \omega\|^2\notag\\
&\leq \|\omega\|\|V_1'' \partial_x \phi - u \cdot \nabla \omega_{a} - Er^1\|+\|\omega\|\| \partial_x\theta\| \\
&\leq \| \omega\|(\|\partial_x \phi\| \|V_1''\|_{L^\infty} + \|u\| \|\nabla \omega_{a}\|_{L^\infty} + \|Er^1\|) +\|\omega\|\| \partial_x\theta\|\\
&\lesssim\|\omega\|^2( \|V_1''\|_{L^\infty} +\|\nabla \omega_{a}\|_{L^\infty}) + \|\omega\|\|Er^1\| + \|\omega\|\| \partial_x\theta\|,
\end{align*}
\begin{align*}
\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rm d}{{\rm d}t} \|\theta\|^2_{L^2}+\nu \|\nabla \theta\|^2
&\leq \|\theta\|\|V_2' \partial_x \phi - u \cdot \nabla \theta_{a} - Er^2\|\\
&\leq \| \theta\|(\|\partial_x \phi\| \|V_2'\|_{L^\infty} + \|u\| \|\nabla \theta_{a}\|_{L^\infty} + \|Er^2\|) \\
&\lesssim\| \theta\|\|\omega\|( \|V_2'\|_{L^\infty} +\|\nabla \theta_{a}\|_{L^\infty}) + \|\theta\|\|Er^2\| .
\end{align*}
Integrating the above equations with respect to $t$ yields
\begin{align}\label{123}
  &\|\omega\|^2_{L^\infty L^2}+\nu \|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2L^2}^2 \notag\\ 
  &\leq C\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}^2( \|V_1''\|_{L^1L^\infty} +\|\nabla \omega_{a}\|_{L^1 L^\infty}) + C\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}(\|Er^1\|_{L^1L^2}+\|\partial_x\theta\|_{L^1L^2}).
\end{align}
\begin{align}\label{456}
  &\|\theta\|^2_{L^\infty L^2}+\nu \|\nabla \theta\|_{L^2L^2}^2 \notag\\ 
  &\leq C\|\theta\|_{L^\infty L^2}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}( \|V_2'\|_{L^1L^\infty} +\|\nabla \theta_{a}\|_{L^1 L^\infty}) + C\|\theta\|_{L^\infty L^2}\|Er^2\|_{L^1L^2}. 
\end{align}
From \eqref{2.3}--\eqref{2.4} and Proposition~\ref{p2.1}, we have
\begin{align*}
&\|\nabla \omega_{a}\|_{L^\infty} + \|V_1''\|_{L^\infty}\leq C (1+t) (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}) \leq C\varepsilon_0 (1+t) \nu^{\frac{1}{3}}, \\
&\|\nabla \theta_{a}\|_{L^\infty} + \|V_2'\|_{L^\infty}\leq C (1+t) \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}\leq C\varepsilon_0 (1+t) \nu^{\frac{5}{6}}, \\
&\|Er^1\| \lesssim \nu (1+t) (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}) + (1+t)^{-2} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4})^2,\\
&\|Er^2\|\lesssim  \nu (1+t)\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} +  (1+t)^{-2}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}(\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}).
\end{align*}
 Then, we arrive at 
 \begin{align}
  &\|\omega\|^2_{L^\infty L^2}+\nu \|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2L^2}^2 \notag\\ 
  \leq& C\varepsilon_0(1+t)^2 \nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}^2 +\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}\|\partial_x\theta\|_{L^1L^2}\notag\\ 
  &+ C\left[\nu (1+t)^2 (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}) + (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4})^2\right]\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2} \notag\\ 
  \leq& C\varepsilon_0\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}^2 +C\left[\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\theta\|_{X_1}+\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}(\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4})\right]\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}.\notag
\end{align}
\begin{align}
 &\|\theta\|_{L^\infty L^2}^2 + \nu \|\nabla \theta\|_{L^2L^2}^2\notag\\  
 \leq& C\varepsilon_0(1+t)^2 \nu^{\frac{5}{6}}\|\theta\|_{L^\infty L^2}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}\notag\\ 
 &+ C\left[\nu (1+t)^2\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} + \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}(\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4})\right]\|\theta\|_{L^\infty L^2}\notag\\
 \leq& C\varepsilon_0\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\theta\|_{L^\infty L^2}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}+C\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}(\nu^{\frac{2}{3}}+\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4})\|\theta\|_{L^\infty L^2}.\notag
\end{align}
By using the smallness of $\varepsilon_0$, we obtain 
 \begin{align}\label{4.12}
  \|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}+\nu^{\frac{1}{2}} \|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2L^2} 
  \leq C\left[\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\theta\|_{X_1}+\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}(\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4})\right],
\end{align}
\begin{align}\label{4.13}
 \|\theta\|_{L^\infty L^2} + \nu^{\frac{1}{2}} \|\nabla \theta\|_{L^2L^2}  
 \leq C\varepsilon_0\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}+C\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}(\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}+\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}).
\end{align}
Notice that 
\begin{align}\label{4.14}
  \nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\theta\|_{X_1}\leq& C_1\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}( \nu^{\frac{2}{3}}+\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4})+C_1\nu^{-\frac{5}{6}}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}\notag\\ 
  &+C_1\nu^{-1} \|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}\|\theta\|_{X_1}+C_1\nu^{-\frac{9}{8}}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}^{\frac{9}{10}}(\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2 L^2})^{\frac{1}{10}}\|\theta\|_{X_1}. \end{align}
  Combining the above three equations gives
\begin{align}\label{4.15a}
&\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}+\nu^{\frac{1}{2}} \|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2L^2}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}} \|\theta\|_{L^\infty L^2}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\theta\|_{X_1}\notag\\ 
\leq&C\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}(\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4})+C\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}\notag\\ 
&+C\nu^{-1} \|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}\|\theta\|_{X_1}+C\nu^{-\frac{9}{8}} \|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}^{\frac{9}{10}}(\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2 L^2})^{\frac{1}{10}}\|\theta\|_{X_1}.
\end{align}
Assume that $\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}+\nu^{\frac{1}{2}} \|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2L^2}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\theta\|_{X_1}\leq \varepsilon_1\nu^{\frac{2}{3}}$ for some small constant $\varepsilon_1$, we conclude
\begin{align}\label{4.15}
\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}+\nu^{\frac{1}{2}} \|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2L^2}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\theta\|_{X_1}\leq C\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}(\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}).
\end{align}
Notice that $\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3} +\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}\leq \varepsilon_0 \nu^{\frac{1}{3}},$
then we have
\[
\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}+\nu^{\frac{1}{2}} \|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2L^2}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}} \|\theta\|_{L^\infty L^2}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\theta\|_{X_1} \leq C(\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}) \leq C\varepsilon_0 \nu^{\frac{2}{3}}.
\]
Taking $\varepsilon_0 = \frac{\varepsilon_1 }{2C}$, then one can get \eqref{4.11} for $0 \leq t \leq\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}$.
Thus, we get \eqref{4.11}. 
The proof of this proposition is completed.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- prop-8: body_md -->
\label{p4.8a}
Suppose that $f$ satisfies \eqref{4.15aa} and $V_1(t,y)$ satisfies \eqref{eq:4.1}, then for $t\geq T_0$, there exists $0 < \delta \ll 1$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{4.15bb}
\|f\|_{X_2} \lesssim \|f(T_0)\| + \|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} g_2\|_{L^1 L^2}.
\end{equation}
<!-- end prop-8 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
The proof of this proposition is similar to Proposition 2.4 in \cite{wei-zhang2026}. For brevity, we
omit the details.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- prop-9: body_md -->
\label{prop:2.6}
The following estimates hold
\begin{align}\label{h1}
\|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} h_1\|_{L^1 L^2} \lesssim& \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}) \|\omega\|_{X_2}+ \nu^{-\frac{5}{8}}\|\omega\|_{X_2}^2 \notag\\
&+\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} \|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}} \|\theta\|_{X_2},
\end{align}
\begin{align}\label{h2}
\|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} h_2\|_{L^1 L^2} \lesssim& \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} \|\omega\|_{X_2}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}) \|\theta\|_{X_2}\notag\\
&+ \nu^{-\frac{5}{8}} \|\omega\|_{X_2}\|\theta\|_{X_2} +\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}.
\end{align}
<!-- end prop-9 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
Let's first prove \eqref{h2}, which will be divided into several steps.

{\it Step 1. The estimate of $V_2' \partial_x \phi$.} By using \eqref{2.4}, we have
\begin{align*}
\|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} V_2' \partial_x \phi\|_{L^1 L^2} &\lesssim \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} \|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} P_{\neq} \omega\|_{L^1 L^2} \lesssim \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} \|\omega\|_{X_2}\\
&\lesssim\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} \|\omega\|_{X_2}.
\end{align*}



{\it Step 2. The estimate of $u_{ob} \cdot \nabla \theta$.} Note that $u_{ob}=u +u_{a}$, thus,
\[
u_{ob} \cdot \nabla \theta = u_{a} \cdot \nabla \theta + u \cdot \nabla \theta.
\]

For the first term, 
\[
u_{a} \cdot \nabla \theta = u_{a}^{(1)} \partial_x \theta + u_{a}^{(2)} \partial_y \theta.
\]
It follows from \eqref{2.13} that
\begin{align*}
\|u_{a}^{(1)}\|_{L^\infty L^\infty} &\lesssim T_0^{-1} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}) \lesssim\nu^{\frac{1}{6}} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}), \\
\|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} u_{a}^{(2)}\|_{L^2 L^\infty} &\lesssim \|t^{-2}\|_{L^2(T_0,\infty)} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4})\\& \lesssim T_0^{-1} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}) \lesssim \nu^{\frac{1}{6}} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}).
\end{align*}
Thus, by Hölder's inequality, we deduce
\begin{align*}
\|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} u_{a} \cdot \nabla \theta\|_{L^1 L^2} &\lesssim \|u_{a}^{(1)}\|_{L^\infty L^\infty} \|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \partial_x \theta\|_{L^1 L^2} + \|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} u_{a}^{(2)}\|_{L^2 L^\infty} \|\nabla \theta\|_{L^2 L^2} \\
&\lesssim \nu^{\frac{1}{6}} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4})\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}} \|\theta\|_{X_2}\\
&\lesssim \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}) \|\theta\|_{X_2}.
\end{align*}
For the second term, 
\[
u \cdot \nabla \theta = P_{\neq} u \cdot \nabla \theta + P_0 u^1 \partial_x \theta.
\]
It follows from Lemma~\ref{lem:A.1} and $u = (\partial_y, -\partial_x) \Delta^{-1} \omega$ that
\begin{align*}
\|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} P_{\neq} u\|_{L^2 L^\infty} &\lesssim \|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \partial_x u\|_{L^2 L^2}^{\frac{3}{4}} \|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \nabla \omega\|_{L^2 L^2}^{\frac{1}{4}} \\
&\lesssim \|\omega\|_{X_2}^{\frac{3}{4}} (\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}} \|\omega\|_{X_2})^{\frac{1}{4}} = \nu^{-\frac{1}{8}} \|\omega\|_{X_2}.
\end{align*}
One can infer that
\begin{align*}
\|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} P_{\neq} u \cdot \nabla \theta\|_{L^1 L^2} &\leq \|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} P_{\neq} u\|_{L^2 L^\infty} \|\nabla \theta\|_{L^2 L^2} \lesssim \nu^{-\frac{1}{8}} \|\omega\|_{X_2} \cdot \nu^{-\frac{1}{2}} \|\theta\|_{X_2} \lesssim \nu^{-\frac{5}{8}}\|\omega\|_{X_2}\|\theta\|_{X_2}, \\
\|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} P_0 u^1 \partial_x \theta\|_{L^1 L^2} &\leq \|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2} \|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \partial_x \theta\|_{L^1 L^2} \lesssim \nu^{-\frac{1}{2}} \|\omega\|_{X_2}\|\theta\|_{X_2}\lesssim \nu^{-\frac{1}{2}} \|\omega\|_{X_2}\|\theta\|_{X_2},
\end{align*}
which imply
\[
\|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} u \cdot \nabla \theta\|_{L^1 L^2} \lesssim \nu^{-\frac{5}{8}} \|\omega\|_{X_2}\|\theta\|_{X_2}.
\]

{\it Step 3. The estimate of $u^{(1)} \partial_x \theta_{a} + u^{(2)} (\partial_y + t \partial_x) \theta_{a}$.}  From Proposition~\ref{p2.1},  one gets
\begin{align*}
\|u^{(2)} (\partial_y + t \partial_x) \theta_{a}\| &\leq \|u^{(2)}\| \|(\partial_y + t \partial_x) \theta_{a}\|_{L^\infty} \lesssim \|\omega\| {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}, \\
\|u^{(1)} \partial_x \theta_{a}\| &\leq \|u^{(1)}\| \|\partial_x \theta_{a}\|_{L^\infty} \lesssim \|\omega\| {\rm e}^{-2\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4},
\end{align*}
which means that
\begin{align*}
\|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} (u^{(1)} \partial_x \theta_{a} + u^{(2)} (\partial_y + t \partial_x) \theta_{a})\|_{L^1 L^2}&\lesssim \|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2} \|{\rm e}^{-\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t}\|_{L^1(T_0,\infty)} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} \\
&  \lesssim \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} \|\omega\|_{X_2}.
\end{align*}

{\it Step 4. The estimate of $Er^2$.} By applying \eqref{2.15}, we obtain
\begin{align*}
\|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} Er^2\|_{L^1 L^2} \lesssim& \|\nu (1+t) {\rm e}^{-\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t}\|_{L^1(T_0,\infty)} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} \\ 
&+ \|(1+t)^{-2}\|_{L^1(T_0,\infty)} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}(\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}) \\
\lesssim& \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} +\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}(\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4})\lesssim \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} .
\end{align*}
By combining the above formulas, we get \eqref{h2}.

Similarly, we can also prove \eqref{h1}.
The proof of this proposition is completed.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- prop-10: body_md -->
\label{p4.12a}
Let $\omega_{re}$ and $\theta_{re}$ be the solution of \eqref{aaa} and \eqref{bbb} respectively, we have 
\begin{align}
&\|\omega_{re}\|_{X_2} \lesssim \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} \|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \nabla u^{(2)}\|_{L^2 L^2} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}),\label{4.34a}\\
&\|\theta_{re}\|_{X_2} \lesssim \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} \|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \nabla u^{(2)}\|_{L^2 L^2} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}.\label{4.35a}
\end{align}
<!-- end prop-10 -->
<!-- lem-8: body_md -->
\label{l4.13}
Suppose that $0<\delta\ll 1$, $f$ is a given function,  there holds 
\[
\|f\|_{X_2} \lesssim \|{\rm e}^{\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t/2} f\|_Y + \nu^{\frac{1}{2}} \|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \partial_x f\|_{L^1 L^2}.
\]
<!-- end lem-8 -->
<!-- lem-9: body_md -->
\label{l4.14}
For $f_4$, we can deduce
\[
\|f_4\|_{X_2} \lesssim \|{\rm e}^{-\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t/2} f_1\|_Y + \|\partial_x f_1\|_{L^\infty L^2}.
\]
<!-- end lem-9 -->
<!-- lem-10: body_md -->
\label{l4.15}
   Suppose $\delta$ is defined in Proposition \ref{p4.8a}, there holds
\begin{equation}\label{4.38}
\|\theta_{re}\|_{X_2} \lesssim \|{\rm e}^{-\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}t/2}f_1\|_Y + \|\partial_x f_1\|_{L^\infty L^2} + \nu^{\frac{1}{6}}\|\partial_x \theta_{re}\|_{X_2}.
\end{equation}
<!-- end lem-10 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
By applying Lemma \ref{l4.13} shows
\[
\|f_3\|_{X_2} \lesssim \|{\rm e}^{\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}t/2}f_3\|_Y + \nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\rm e}^{\delta\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_x f_3\|_{L^1 L^2}.
\]
It follows from \eqref{4.36a} that
\[
\|{\rm e}^{\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}t/2}f_3\|_Y \leq \|f_2\|_Y + \|{\rm e}^{-\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}t/2}f_1\|_Y \lesssim \|{\rm e}^{-\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}t/2}f_1\|_Y.
\]
Thus, one gets
\[
\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\rm e}^{\delta\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_x f_3\|_{L^1 L^2} \leq \nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\rm e}^{\delta\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_x f_4\|_{L^1 L^2} + \nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\rm e}^{\delta\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}t}\partial_x \theta_{re}\|_{L^1 L^2} \leq \|f_4\|_{X_2} + \nu^{\frac{1}{6}}\|\partial_x \theta_{re}\|_{X_2},
\]
and then,
\[
\|f_3\|_{X_2} \lesssim \|{\rm e}^{-\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}t/2}f_1\|_Y + \|f_4\|_{X_2} + \nu^{\frac{1}{6}}\|\partial_x \theta_{re}\|_{X_2},
\]
which along with Lemma \ref{l4.14} gives \eqref{4.38}.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- lem-11: body_md -->
\label{l4.16}
Let $\theta_{re}$ be the solution of \eqref{bbb}, then there holds
\[
\|\partial_x\theta_{re}\|_{X_2} \lesssim \nu^{-\frac{1}{2}} \|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \nabla u^{(2)}\|_{L^2 L^2} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}.
\]
<!-- end lem-11 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
By calculating $\partial_x\eqref{bbb}$, 
\[
\partial_t \partial_x \theta_{re}  + V_1 \partial_x^2 \theta_{re}- \nu \Delta \partial_x \theta_{re} - t \partial_x (u^{(2)} \partial_x \theta_{a}) = 0, \quad \partial_x \theta_{re}|_{y=\pm 1} = \partial_x \theta_{re}|_{t=T_0} = 0.
\]
Using Proposition~\ref{p4.8a} gives
\[
\|\partial_x \theta_{re}\|_{X_2} \lesssim \|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} t\partial_x (u^{(2)} \partial_x \theta_{a})\|_{L^1 L^2}.
\]
Notice that  $P_0 u^{(2)} = 0$, $\|u^{(2)}\| \leq \|\partial_x u^{(2)}\| \leq \|\nabla u^{(2)}\|$, by Lemma \ref{l3.2}, we deduce
\begin{align*}
\|t \partial_x (u^{(2)} \partial_x \theta_{a})\| &\leq t \|\partial_x^2 \theta_{a}\|_{L^\infty} \|u^{(2)}\| + t \|\partial_x \theta_{a}\|_{L^\infty} \|\partial_x u^{(2)}\| \\
&\lesssim \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} {\rm e}^{-\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} (\|u^{(2)}\| + \|\partial_x u^{(2)}\|) \lesssim \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} {\rm e}^{-\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} \|\nabla u^{(2)}\|,
\end{align*}
and then,
\begin{align*}
\|\partial_x \theta_{re}\|_{X_2} &\lesssim \|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} t \partial_x (u^{(2)} \partial_x \theta_{a})\|_{L^1 L^2} \lesssim \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} \|{\rm e}^{(\delta-1)\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \nabla u^{(2)}\|_{L^1 L^2} \\
&\leq \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} \|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \nabla u^{(2)}\|_{L^2 L^2} \|{\rm e}^{-\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t}\|_{L^2(T_0,\infty)} \\
&\leq \nu^{-\frac{1}{2}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} \|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \nabla u^{(2)}\|_{L^2 L^2}.
\end{align*}
The proof of Lemma \ref{l4.16} is completed.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- lem-12: body_md -->
\label{l4.17}
It holds that
\[
\|{\rm e}^{-\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t/2} f_1\|_Y + \|\partial_x f_1\|_{L^\infty L^2} \lesssim \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} \|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \nabla u^{(2)}\|_{L^2 L^2} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}.
\]
<!-- end lem-12 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
See Lemma 5.5 in \cite{wei-zhang2026}.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
Thanks to \eqref{4.11} in Proposition~\ref{p4.8}, we derive
\[
\|\omega_*(T_0)\| = \|\omega(T_0)\| \lesssim \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} \|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4},
\]
\begin{equation}\label{4.43}
   \|\theta_*(T_0)\| = \|\theta(T_0)\| \lesssim \nu^{\frac{5}{6}} \|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}.
\end{equation}
Then it follows from \eqref{4.43}, Proposition~\ref{p4.8a} and Proposition~\ref{prop:2.6} that
\begin{align}\label{4.44}
\|\omega_*\|_{X_2} \lesssim& \|\omega_*(T_0)\| + \|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} h_1\|_{L^1 L^2} \notag\\
\lesssim& \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}) \|\omega\|_{X_2}+ \nu^{-\frac{5}{8}}\|\omega\|_{X_2}^2 \notag\\
&+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}} \|\theta\|_{X_2}+\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} \|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4},
\end{align}
\begin{align}\label{4.45}
\|\theta_*\|_{X_2} \lesssim& \|\theta_*(T_0)\| + \|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} h_2\|_{L^1 L^2} \notag\\
\lesssim& \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} \|\omega\|_{X_2}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}) \|\theta\|_{X_2}+ \nu^{-\frac{5}{8}}\|\omega\|_{X_2}\|\theta\|_{X_2} \notag\\
&+\nu^{\frac{5}{6}} \|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}.
\end{align}
Moreover, thanks to 
\[
\|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \nabla u^2\|_{L^2 L^2} = \|{\rm e}^{\delta \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} t} \partial_x \nabla \Delta^{-1} \omega\|_{L^2 L^2} \leq \|\omega\|_{X_2},
\]
then using $\omega=\omega_*+\omega_{re}$, Proposition~\ref{p4.12a} and \eqref{4.44}--\eqref{4.45} gives
\begin{align}\label{4.46}
\|\omega\|_{X_2} \leq \|\omega_*\|_{X_2} + \|\omega_{re}\|_{X_2}\lesssim&\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}) \|\omega\|_{X_2}+ \nu^{-\frac{5}{8}}\|\omega\|_{X_2}^2 \notag\\
&+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}} \|\theta\|_{X_2}+\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} \|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}, 
\end{align}
\begin{align}\label{4.47}
\|\theta\|_{X_2} \leq \|\theta_*\|_{X_2} + \|\theta_{re}\|_{X_2} \lesssim& \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} \|\omega\|_{X_2}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}) \|\theta\|_{X_2}\notag\\
&+ \nu^{-\frac{5}{8}} \|\omega\|_{X_2}\|\theta\|_{X_2} +\nu^{\frac{5}{6}} \|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}. 
\end{align}
By calculating $\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\cdot$\eqref{4.47}+$\bar{\varepsilon}\cdot$\eqref{4.46}~($0<\bar{\varepsilon}\ll 1$), and noting that the linear term $\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}} \|\theta\|_{X_2}$ is absorbed due to the smallness of $\bar{\varepsilon}$, we get
\begin{align}
  \|\omega\|_{X_2}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\theta\|_{X_2} \lesssim&\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}) \|\omega\|_{X_2}+ \nu^{-\frac{5}{8}}\|\omega\|_{X_2}^2 \notag\\
&+\nu^{-\frac{5}{6}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} \|\omega\|_{X_2}+\nu^{-\frac{5}{6}} (\|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}) \|\theta\|_{X_2}\notag\\
&+ \nu^{-\frac{9}{8}} \|\omega\|_{X_2}\|\theta\|_{X_2}+\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} \|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4}  .\notag
\end{align}
Then using the condition \eqref{1.4}, we arrive at
\begin{align}\label{4.48}
  \|\omega\|_{X_2}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\theta\|_{X_2} \leq&C(\nu^{-\frac{5}{8}}\|\omega\|_{X_2}^2 + \nu^{-\frac{9}{8}} \|\omega\|_{X_2}\|\theta\|_{X_2}+\nu^{\frac{1}{3}} \|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}\|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4})  .
\end{align}
With the energy estimate \eqref{4.48} at hand, we are ready to set up the bootstrap argument.
For $T_0\leq t\leq T$, we first assume that $\|\omega\|_{X_2} \leq \varepsilon_1 \nu^{\frac{2}{3}}, \|\theta\|_{X_2} \leq \varepsilon_1 \nu^{\frac{7}{6}}$ for a constant $0<\varepsilon_1\ll 1$ which will be determined later. Then by \eqref{4.48}, we have
\begin{equation}\label{4.49}
\|\omega\|_{X_2}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\theta\|_{X_2} \leq C \nu^{\frac{1}{3}} \|\Omega_{in}\|_{H^3}+C\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}} \|\Theta_{in}\|_{H^4} \leq C \varepsilon_0 \nu^{\frac{2}{3}}.
\end{equation}
Taking $\varepsilon_0 = \frac{\varepsilon_1 }{2C}$, then one can get \eqref{4.49} for all $t\geq T_0$. Combining Proposition~\ref{p4.8} and \eqref{4.49}, and noticing the definition of $\|\cdot\|_{X_2} $ in \eqref{4.15aaa}, we obtain \eqref{222} and hence complete the proof of
Proposition \ref{p2.2}.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- prop-11: body_md -->
\label{ap3.5}
Suppose that $u_{a}$ is defined as in \eqref{a3.3}, and $f$ solves
\begin{equation}\label{a3.50}
\left\{
\begin{aligned}
&\partial_t f - \nu \Delta f + y \partial_x f+u_{a}\cdot\nabla f = 0, \ \ \ \  t>0,\\
&f|_{t=0}=f_{in}(x,y),\quad f(t,x,\pm 1) = 0, \quad \nabla\cdot u_{a}=0,
\end{aligned}
\right.
\end{equation}
then for $0<t\leq \nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}$, it holds that 
\begin{align*}
&\|k f_k(t)\|_{L^2_y} \lesssim (\nu t)^{-\frac{1}{2}} (1+t)^{-1} \|f_{in,k}\|_{L^2_y}, \\
&\|f_{k}(t)\|_{L^2_y} \lesssim (1 +\nu t+ \nu k^2t^3)^{-\frac{1}{2}} \|f_{in,k}\|_{L^2_y}.
\end{align*}
Here $f_k$ is the Fourier transform of $f$ with respect to the variable $x$ and $k\in\mathbb{R}$.
<!-- end prop-11 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
The proof is similar to that of Proposition \ref{p4.1},  we omit the details here.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- coro-2: body_md -->
\label{ac3.6}
    Under the conditions of Proposition \ref{ap3.5}, we have for $t\leq \nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}$ that
  \begin{align*}
\|f(t)\| + \nu^{\frac{1}{2}} \left(\int_0^t \|\nabla f(s)\|^2 {\rm d} s\right)^{\frac{1}{2}} + \nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_0^t \|\partial_x f(s)\|{\rm d} s\lesssim \|f_{in}\|.
\end{align*}
<!-- end coro-2 -->
<!-- prop-12: body_md -->
\label{ap3.7}
Suppose that $(\omega,\theta)$ is the solution to \eqref{a3.5}, then for $0\leq t\leq T_0=\nu^{-\frac{1}{6}}$,
\begin{align}\label{a3.51}
  \|\theta\|_{X_1}\lesssim& \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}\mathcal{E}_{2,in}+\nu^{-\frac{7}{12}}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}\|\theta\|_{X_1}\notag \\ 
  &+\nu^{-\frac{5}{8}}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}^{\frac{9}{10}}(\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2 L^2})^{\frac{1}{10}}\|\theta\|_{X_1}+\nu^{\frac13}\ln(1+\nu^{-1})\mathcal{E}_{2,in}.  
\end{align}
<!-- end prop-12 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
Notice that
\begin{equation}\notag
    \partial_t \theta - \nu \Delta \theta + y \partial_x \theta+u_{a}\cdot\nabla\theta=g_1, \ \theta|_{t=0}=0,
\end{equation}
where 
\begin{equation}\notag
    g_1=- u \cdot \nabla \theta_{a}- u \cdot \nabla \theta  -Er^2.
\end{equation}
From Corollary \ref{ac3.6} and Duhamel's principle, we have 
\begin{equation}\label{a3.52}
\|\theta\|_{X_1} \lesssim \left\| g_1 \right\|_{L^1 L^2}.
\end{equation}
Now we begin to estimate $\left\| g_1 \right\|_{L^1 L^2}$. 
Firstly, it follows from \eqref{a3.8}--\eqref{a3.9} that
\begin{align}
\|u \cdot \nabla \theta_{a}\| \lesssim \|u\| \|\nabla\theta_{a}\|_{L^\infty}\lesssim t\|\omega\|\mathcal{E}_{2,in},\notag
\end{align}
and then,
\begin{align}\label{a3.53}
\|u \cdot \nabla \theta_{a}\|_{L^1 L^2} \lesssim t^2\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}\mathcal{E}_{2,in}\lesssim \nu^{-\frac{1}{3}}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}\mathcal{E}_{2,in}.
\end{align}
Next, notice that
\[
u \cdot \nabla \theta = u^{(1)} \partial_x \theta + u^{(2)} \partial_y \theta.
\]
Then 
\begin{align}
  \|u^{(1)} \partial_x \theta\|\lesssim \|u^{(1)}\|_{L^\infty}\|\partial_x \theta\|\lesssim \int_{\mathbb{R}}\|u^{(1)}_k\|_{L^\infty_y}{\rm d} k \|\partial_x \theta\|.
\end{align}
For $|k|\leq 1$, we use Sobolev inequality to obtain
\begin{equation}\notag
  \int_{|k|\leq 1}\|u^{(1)}_k\|_{L^\infty_y}{\rm d} k\leq \int_{|k|\leq 1}\|\omega_k\|_{L^2_y}{\rm d} k\lesssim \|\omega_k\|_{L^2_kL^2_y}\lesssim \|\omega\|_{L^2}.  
\end{equation}
For $|k|\geq 1$, 
\begin{align}
  \int_{|k|\geq 1}\|u^{(1)}_k\|_{L^\infty_y}{\rm d} k\leq& \int_{|k|\geq 1}\|u^{(1)}_k\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^2_y}\|\partial_yu^{(1)}_k\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^2_y}{\rm d} k=\int_{|k|\geq 1}|k|^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\delta_1}{2}}\|k^{1+\delta_1}u^{(1)}_k\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^2_y}\|\partial_yu^{(1)}_k\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^2_y}{\rm d} k\notag \\ 
  \lesssim& \||k|^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\delta_1}{2}}\|_{L^2_k(|k|\geq 1)}\|k^{1+\delta_1}u^{(1)}_k\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^2_kL^2_y}\|\partial_yu^{(1)}_k\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^2_kL^2_y}\notag \\ \lesssim& \|ku^{(1)}_k\|_{L^2_kL^2_y}^{\frac{1-\delta_1}{2}}\|k^2u^{(1)}_k\|_{L^2_kL^2_y}^{\frac{\delta_1}{2}}\|(k,\partial_y)u^{(1)}_k\|_{L^2_kL^2_y}^{\frac{1}{2}} 
  \lesssim \|\omega\|_{L^2}^{1-\frac{\delta_1}{2}}\|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2}^{\frac{\delta_1}{2}}. \notag 
\end{align}
Thus, we deduce
\begin{align}
  \|u^{(1)} \partial_x \theta\|_{ L^1L^2}\lesssim& \|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}\|\partial_x \theta\|_{L^1 L^2}+\left\|\|\omega\|_{L^2}^{1-\frac{\delta_1}{2}}\|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2}^{\frac{\delta_1}{2}}\right\|_{L^2_t}\|\partial_x \theta\|_{L^2 L^2}\notag\\
  \lesssim&\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}\|\partial_x \theta\|_{L^1 L^2}+\|\omega\|_{L^2 L^2}^{1-\frac{\delta_1}{2}} \|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2 L^2}^{\frac{\delta_1}{2}}\|\nabla \theta\|_{L^2 L^2}\notag\\
  \lesssim&\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}\|\partial_x \theta\|_{L^1 L^2}+\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}^{1-\frac{\delta_1}{2}}t^{\frac{1}{2}(1-\frac{\delta_1}{2})}\|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2 L^2}^{\frac{\delta_1}{2}}\|\nabla \theta\|_{L^2 L^2}\notag\\ 
  \lesssim&\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}\|\partial_x \theta\|_{L^1 L^2}+\nu^{-\frac{1}{12}+\frac{\delta_1}{24}}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}^{1-\frac{\delta_1}{2}}\|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2 L^2}^{\frac{\delta_1}{2}}\|\nabla \theta\|_{L^2 L^2}.\notag
\end{align}
Let $\delta_1=\frac{1}{5}$, then
\begin{align}\label{a3.55}
  \|u^{(1)} \partial_x \theta\|_{ L^1L^2}\lesssim& \nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}(\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\partial_x \theta\|_{L^1 L^2})+\nu^{-\frac{5}{8}}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}^{\frac{9}{10}}(\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2 L^2})^{\frac{1}{10}}(\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla \theta\|_{L^2 L^2})\notag\\ 
  \lesssim& \nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}\|\theta\|_{X_1}+\nu^{-\frac{5}{8}}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}^{\frac{9}{10}}(\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2 L^2})^{\frac{1}{10}}\|\theta\|_{X_1}. 
\end{align}
For $\|u^{(2)} \partial_y \theta\|_{ L^1L^2}$, note that when $|k|\leq 1$,
\begin{equation}\notag
   \|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}\|\partial_y \theta\|_{L^1 L^2}\lesssim \|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2} t^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla \theta\|_{L^2 L^2}\lesssim \nu^{-\frac{7}{12}}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}(\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla \theta\|_{L^2 L^2}), 
\end{equation}
and when $|k|\geq 1$, we use a similar approach as before to calculate, and ultimately obtain
\begin{align}\label{a3.56}
  \|u^{(2)} \partial_y \theta\|_{ L^1L^2}\lesssim& \nu^{-\frac{7}{12}}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}(\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla \theta\|_{L^2 L^2})+\nu^{-\frac{5}{8}}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}^{\frac{9}{10}}(\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2 L^2})^{\frac{1}{10}}(\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla \theta\|_{L^2 L^2})\notag\\ 
  \lesssim& \nu^{-\frac{7}{12}}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}\|\theta\|_{X_1}+\nu^{-\frac{5}{8}}\|\omega\|_{L^\infty L^2}^{\frac{9}{10}}(\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla \omega\|_{L^2 L^2})^{\frac{1}{10}}\|\theta\|_{X_1}. 
\end{align}
For $\|Er^2\|_{L^1 L^2}$,
